CompactDatabase recomputes the exact value (3 rows remained after the deletes and refusals: the compacted file reads 2, 3 and 3 for the three indexes, against 4, 6 and 5 before). ACCDB and MDB give the same sequence.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, Indexes refreshed before each read, a primary key, a non-unique and a unique index, SQL and recordset writes.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dc-rules-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: the counts after each of 11 steps, after reopen and after compaction are identical in both formats); `evidence/r-fmt-2b/ix-delete-max-key/`, `ix-delete-low-range/`, `ix-delete-reinsert-ladder/`, `ix-dup-ladder/`, `dr-reinsert-same-key-same-values/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/idx-key-ladder/`, `upd-indexed-value/`. Discriminators: duplicate, delete of a duplicate, delete of the last row of a key, reinsert, update away and back, refused insert, cancel and refused update each a separate step.
- **Open**: refusal order for indexes other than these three; the physical word is bytes (T); `DistinctCount` is never used as a row count.

### R-FMT-2 Compact & Repair diagnostic and R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-COMPACT: CompactDatabase leaves the source unchanged and the copy reads the same public data, except for the counters it recomputes

- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CompactDatabase(source, work file)` returns hr 0, leaves the source file's hash unchanged, and the compacted file reads the same typed rows, field metadata and index lists as the source in eight probes; compacted sizes vary (475136 B from 503808 B after a Memo shrink, equal after growth). It recomputes two counters: the index `DistinctCount` and the AutoNumber seed (see their notes). In the catalog view the only differences are `Database.Name` and, later, `LastUpdated` of some tables and their Containers documents.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (MDB for the statistics probe), work file written next to the attempt, source closed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2b/compact-after-fixed-update/`, `compact-after-page-growth/`, `compact-after-page-emptied/`, `compact-after-memo-shrink/`, `compact-after-memo-growth/`, `compact-after-ole-insert/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-compact/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2b-compact-after-fixed-update/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/auto-compact-accdb/`, `dc-rules-accdb/`, `dc-rules-mdb/`.
- **Open**: Compact after a rolled-back or refused operation; repair-marked Compact & Repair (Access).

### R-FMT-2 Failed operations (refusal identity): duplicate, validation, NOT NULL and foreign-key refusals have stable numbers

- **Claim**: a duplicate in a unique or primary index is refused with 0x800A0BCE (3022), a Null in an index that disallows Nulls or in a primary key with 3058, a Null in a NOT NULL column with 3314, a validation-rule violation with 3316 and the rule's ValidationText ("P must be positive"), a missing parent row with 3201 ("The related record is required in table 'Parent'"). An omitted column takes its default.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, `Execute` with dbFailOnError and per-statement Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-accdb/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/` (3022), `fail-hash-fk/` (3201), `fail-hash-validation/` (3316), `dao-index-primary-accdb/`. Discriminators: each kind on its own statement; both formats.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-DATA-EDITS: ordinary data edits leave every DAO-visible catalog object and property untouched

- **Claim**: a row edit, a row growth that relocates it, an LV replacement in a chain, an LV delete with retirement and a pressure insert of 400 rows change the file hash and change nothing in the DAO catalog view (table, query, relation and Containers names, properties, `LastUpdated`), apart from the edited table's `RecordCount`. A read/write open and close with no statement leaves the hash and the view identical.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, an engine-created baseline with tables, relations, saved queries, user and unknown properties; the view taken before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-row-edit/`, `pres-grow-relocate/`, `pres-lv-replace-chain/`, `pres-lv-delete-retire/`, `pres-ins-pressure/`, `pres-noop/` (pres4 attempts; the pres3 ones are stale); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/noop-rw-accdb/`. Discriminator: the same view does show property and DDL edits (next note).
- **Open**: protected-range byte comparison (W-04, T); an Access-created front-end baseline (T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-PROPERTIES and PRES-CATALOG (public view): each property edit and each DDL edit shows as exactly its own change

- **Claim**: four property edits made one at a time are each visible in the view and change no other property and not `T.LastUpdated`; `CREATE INDEX` grows the file 4096 B and changes nothing visible; add and drop column change only the changed table's `LastUpdated`; `CREATE TABLE` grows the file 12288 B, adds one TableDef, one Containers document, and increments the MSysACEs and MSysObjects record counts.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB; DAO property edits and SQL DDL each as its own step, view before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`, `pres-catalog-ladder/` (pres4 attempts).
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-QUERY-PROPS: a row-returning single-table SELECT lists the table's properties among its own; a join and an action query do not

- **Claim**: with a table T that has two table properties (`Description` and an unknown `CalcDbUnknown`), the saved queries `SELECT * FROM T`, with a WHERE, with ORDER BY, `SELECT DISTINCT` and an aggregate (`COUNT(*)`) each list 16 properties including both, while a join of T and U, an UPDATE of T and a SELECT over U (which has none) list 14 properties without them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `CreateQueryDef`, Properties of each QueryDef enumerated after Refresh.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/query-props-accdb/` (re-read: names and counts); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`. Discriminators: WHERE, ORDER BY, DISTINCT, aggregate, join, action query each as its own query.
- **Open**: a single-table SELECT over a table that has properties other than T (only one such table was asked).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-RENAME: an engine-only table rename follows into relationships but not into saved queries

- **Claim**: renaming a table through `TableDef.Name` updates the `Table` of a relationship that references it (R1 to R1x, R5 to R5x) and leaves every saved query's SQL unchanged, so a query over the renamed table then cannot be opened (`OpenRecordset` of `SELECT * FROM R3;` fails after R3 is renamed, and the one over R5 after R5); renaming a table that nothing references changes nothing else; the queries over tables not yet renamed still open.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, each of four renames its own step (referenced by a relationship only, by a query only, by nothing, by both); relations, query SQL, table names and an open of each query read after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/rename-variants-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-rename-referenced/`. Discriminators: the four referencing kinds one at a time.
- **Open**: Access Name AutoCorrect (Access only; T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-ROLLBACK-FAILED: a refusal after index work and a rolled-back insert change the file bytes; a validation refusal, a NOT NULL refusal and a rolled-back update do not

- **Claim**: on copies of one fixture, a refused duplicate in a unique index and a refused foreign-key insert each change the file hash (size constant), the same refused statement again changes it again, and an open and close afterwards changes nothing; a refused validation-rule insert changes nothing, twice. A transaction that inserts one row and is rolled back changes the hash, a rolled-back bulk insert of 3600 rows of about 200 characters changes it again (size constant, row count 0); the committed bulk insert grows the file from 282624 B to 1945600 B. The earlier results fit: the NOT NULL and validation refusals left the hash equal, and a rolled-back UPDATE left it equal.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, copies of one fixture, each step a read/write open, the statement, close and a hash; Workspaces(0) for the transactions.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/`, `fail-hash-fk/`, `fail-hash-validation/`, `rollback-size-accdb/` (re-read: hashes 7fdc... then 57af..., c2fd... for the unique refusal; e133..., 7c52... for the foreign key; unchanged for validation; 19ed..., f910... for the rollbacks); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`, `txn-ladder/`. Discriminators: refusal kind varied with the fixture fixed; insert versus update rollback; one row versus 3600.
- **Open**: the cause (a statistics word or counter written by the index work is the candidate that fits all records; needs page images of a refusal or rollback, W-04, T; the sampling recorder `evidence/r-sample-rec/` captured successful inserts only: data pages first, then pages 0, 1 and 45-48 at commit, an implicit INSERT written after it returned).

### R-SORT-3 Descriptor dispatch (file version): dbVersion 64 and 128 files share one order, a dbVersion 256 file orders supplementary-plane characters differently

- **Claim**: with the same locale, corpus and index, the dbVersion 64 MDB and the dbVersion 128 ACCDB give identical GROUP BY classes, ORDER BY order and index walks (18 of 40 values, among them the supplementary-plane characters U+1D11E, U+1D400, U+1F600, U+20000, U+20001, U+10000, U+10FFFF, U+3400, U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF, U+1E9E and the lone surrogates, equal the empty string); the dbVersion 256 file ("14.0") gives the supplementary-plane characters, U+3400, U+E000 and the lone surrogates their own classes, while "", " ", U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF and U+1E9E still equal one another; the order differs correspondingly. In all three files an indexed table and an unindexed copy agree, and CollatingOrder is 1033.
- **Conditions**: one 41-value corpus, files made with 64, 128 and 256, the same locale; reads by GROUP BY, ORDER BY K, ID, and index walks forward and backward.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/`, `v256-corpus-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3/create-version-256/`, `equality-accdb/`. Discriminators: file version varied with everything else fixed; indexed versus unindexed.
- **Open**: which other characters differ between 128 and 256 (only this corpus); General97 and General - Legacy selection (T).

### R-SORT-3 Text levels: General Text keys fall into stable equality classes, ordered by a compare that SQL, Seek, GROUP BY and unique indexes share (dbVersion 64 and 128)

- **Claim**: on a General-sorted dbVersion 64 or 128 file, SQL `=`, GROUP BY, DISTINCT, Seek, a unique index and the index walk partition Text values into the same classes: case and kana/width variants are one class (A, a, U+FF21, U+FF41; U+3042, U+30A2, U+FF71), é = É = e + U+0301, ligatures equal their expansions (U+FB01 = "fi", U+0153 = "oe", æ = "ae"), "ss" = "SS" but ß is distinct from "ss", è, å, ö, ø, ü, ı and İ are their own classes, Null and "" are separate. Order is Null first, then the class order (" a" < "1" < "10" < "2" < a-class < å < "a b" < aa < ab < "a-b" < ae-class < b < c < ch < cz < d < e < é-class < è < ... < ss-class < u < ü < ue < z < kana < Han), identical on an indexed and an unindexed table, between fresh and incrementally maintained indexes, between ACCDB and MDB and between 0x0409 and 0x0809. A unique index keeps the first row of each class. Within a class the index walk lists rows in insertion order forward and the exact reverse backward; SQL ORDER BY K without ID lists a class's rows in the reverse of insertion order and ORDER BY K DESC keeps that same relative order (see the tie-order note).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB with General collation; DAO table walks, SQL, ADO; two corpora (203 rows with 125 classes; 54 rows with the card's list).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `order-index-*/`, `order-sql-*/`, `seek-*/`, `maintain-*/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-walk/`, `lg-sql-order/`, `lg-unique-survivors/`, `lu-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-*/`, `sort3b-unique-refusals-lg/`, `-lu/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/` (indexed equals unindexed, MDB equals ACCDB). Discriminators: indexed versus unindexed, fresh versus incremental, unique versus non-unique, two locales, two formats.
- **Open**: LIKE depends on the index (own note). Weights and bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 Positions and controls (public equality): NUL and trailing spaces carry no weight, most other controls and white space do

- **Claim**: in a General key (dbVersion 128) an embedded, leading or trailing U+0000 is stored as given but ignored ("a" NUL "b" = "ab"; "a" NUL and NUL "a" equal "a"); trailing ordinary spaces are ignored ("a", "a ", "a  ") while a leading space or a leading tab is a different key sorting before "a"; U+0001, U+001F, U+007F after "a", the soft hyphen, a no-break space, tab, LF, CR, CR LF, a zero-width space, U+2028 and U+3000 after "a" each make their own class (the controls sort just after "a", then the soft hyphen; the ideographic space, "a b", the no-break space, tab, LF, CR and U+2028 sort among the punctuation before "ab"); a decomposed acute equals the precomposed one and case ("a" + U+0301 = "A" + U+0301 = á); two different combining marks give the same class in either order; a leading combining mark is its own class. An indexed table and an unindexed copy agree.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (controls probe) and ACCDB/MDB (equality probes), counted BSTRs.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/controls-accdb/` (re-read: 32 values, GROUP BY classes and ORDER BY on both tables); `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/create-lg/`. Discriminators: each unit appended to the same base letter one at a time.
- **Open**: which unit advances a position or sets the secondary weight is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 UTF-16 boundaries (field length and stored units): the 255-unit Text limit counts UTF-16 units and lone surrogates are stored as given

- **Claim**: a Text(255) key of 255 UTF-16 units (253 letters plus a pair, or 254 letters plus one unit, including a lone high surrogate) is accepted and one of 256 units is refused with 3163; the refused row stays as an ID-only row. A pair counts two units, a lone surrogate one (LEN), and the stored units are the units written. A Seek `=` for a 256-unit key gives NoMatch. In dbVersion 64 and 128 files A x 253 equals A x 253 + U+20000 and A x 254 equals A x 254 + U+D840, while A x 253, A x 254 and A x 255 are mutually distinct; in a dbVersion 256 file the supplementary character has weight (see Descriptor dispatch, file version).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, counted BSTR puts through a table recordset.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `seek-accdb/`, `create-corpus-*/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/text-units-accdb/`, `-mdb/` (rows 9 and 10: LEN 255); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v256-corpus-accdb/`.
- **Open**: mixed row compression is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Typed key order (Integer and Money, Floating and DATE, NUMERIC / GUID): typed index walks follow numeric order with Nulls first; -0 and +0 are separate keys, and a -0 predicate depends on the index

- **Claim**: forward walks of single-column indexes list Null rows first and then Byte 0, 1, 127, 128, 255; Integer -32768 to 32767; Long minimum to maximum; Currency in numeric order; Decimal(18,4) in numeric order; Date by its double value (-1.5 < -1 < -0.5 < -0 < +0 < 0.5 < 1.5). Single and Double follow the sign-magnitude IEEE order: the negative-sign NaN first, -Infinity, finite values with -0 before +0, +Infinity, then positive-sign NaNs (Double; Single's one NaN sorts first). GUIDs order by the textual hexadecimal digits left to right: 16 GUIDs with the byte 0x01 at each of the 16 byte positions rank from the last textual byte (smallest) to the first (largest), a byte 0x80 at a position ranks just above 0x01 at the same position, all-zero is smallest and all-FF largest, and SQL ORDER BY G gives the same order. -0 and +0 are two values in GROUP BY, DISTINCT, ORDER BY and the index, but without an index the predicates `D = 0`, `D > 0` and `D < 0` treat them as equal (`= 0` returns both rows, `< 0` none), and with an index on D `= 0` returns only +0 and `< 0` returns -0; Single compares them equal (no index). The backward walks of Double and Currency are the exact reverses. ACCDB and MDB agree.
- **Conditions**: one index per column built after the rows, table-recordset walk reading ID; values by bit pattern; the -0 probe reads the same predicates before and after `CREATE INDEX`.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/` (re-read: the forward walks and the backward IX_D and IX_C), `create-nums-mdb/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-nums-decimal-accdb/`, `-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/`, `mix-order-r/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/guid-order-accdb/` (re-read: walk 1 17 23 16 15 14 ... 2 18 24 and the same SQL order), `neg-zero-accdb/` (re-read). Discriminators: NaN of both signs and -0 separately; every GUID byte position one at a time; the same predicate before and after the index.
- **Open**: the DATETIME2 (extended date) index does not order chronologically: with values written by two routes the walk and ORDER BY V give the order 14 15 | 101 102 103 1 2 3 | 104..110 | 111..113 | 4..10 | 11..13, rows of the SQL-literal route ahead of equal or later values of the put route; the cause is not established and needs key bytes (T). Descending numeric indexes; key bytes and the legacy versus corrected NUMERIC mode are T.

### R-SORT-3 Null policy / aliases (public): a Null never collides, an all-Null tuple can be left out of the index, and the refusals are 3022 and 3058

- **Claim**: a plain unique index on K keeps every Null-K row; a compound unique (K, N) keeps rows with a Null in either field and refuses only repeated non-null tuples; `IGNORE NULL` on a single column keeps Null rows out of the index walk, on a compound it leaves out only all-Null tuples; `DISALLOW NULL` and a primary key refuse a Null with 3058 (also at creation on existing Null rows, where no index is left); duplicates refuse with 3022; a NOT NULL column refuses with 3314. DAO indexes made by property combination, setting each property only when True: Primary reads back Unique and Required as well; Unique + Required refuses a duplicate (3022) and a Null (3058); Unique + IgnoreNulls admits Nulls and refuses a duplicate; Required alone admits duplicates and refuses Nulls; Unique + Required + IgnoreNulls still refuses Nulls (Required wins); a Primary index appended to a table that already has a primary key is refused with 3283.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB for the NP tables); refusals read with dbFailOnError and a marked Errors.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-existing-nulls-isolated/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3-np-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/nulls-walk-*`, `nulls-survivors/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dao-index-primary-accdb/` (re-read: seven tables, flags and survivors). Discriminators: each property combination on its own table; before-rows versus after-rows builds.
- **Open**: plan eligibility of IGNORE NULL (needs a plan view, T). Aliases sharing one tree are bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (Seek operators): Seek lands on the first or last row of a group by a fixed rule

- **Claim**: on a Text index with duplicate classes, Seek `=`, `>=` and `<=` land on the first row of the equal group in index order, `>` on the first row of the next group, `<` on the last row of the previous group, `=` on an absent key sets NoMatch (a later read fails with 3021), and `>=` of an absent key lands on the next present key; a Long, Integer, Byte, Double, Single or Currency key equal to 1 finds the text key "1", Empty and Null find the Null row, Boolean True and Date 1.5 with `=` give NoMatch, two keys on a one-column index give 0x800A0CB2. ADO adSeekFirstEQ lands on the lowest class ID, adSeekLastEQ the highest. A Null component for a Yes/No key raises 0x800A0C5A.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB twin), table-type recordset on a named index, ADO 10.0 over Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0 for the ADO lane. The "first row of the group" is the first in the index's tie order, that is insertion order (tie-order note); the earlier "lowest ID" equals it only because those tables were filled in ID order.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/seek-accdb/`, `seek-mdb/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-seek-ops/`, `lg-seek-coercion/`, `lu-seek-ops/`, `mix-seek-*/`. Discriminators: each operator and key type separately; descending index varied.
- **Open**: Seek on a long key (the earlier skipped `>=` for the é x 255 group) belongs to the Length510 note; cursor state transitions are R-CURSOR-1.

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (tie order): equal keys walk in insertion order, backward in the reverse, and SQL ORDER BY lists them in the reverse of insertion order

- **Claim**: with equal keys inserted with IDs in a scrambled order (5, 3, 9, 1, 7 for key "a"; 8, 2, 6 for "b"), the index walk lists 5 3 9 1 7 8 2 6 4 (insertion order within each key), the backward walk is the exact reverse, an index built before the rows and one built after give the same walk, and SQL `ORDER BY K` on the indexed tables and on an unindexed copy lists 7 1 9 3 5 6 2 8 4 (each key's rows in reverse insertion order) with `ORDER BY K DESC` keeping the same relative order inside a key (4 6 2 8 7 1 9 3 5) and `ORDER BY K, ID` ordering by ID. After deleting IDs 3 and 7 and inserting IDs 3 and 11 (key "a"), the walk is 5 9 1 3 11: the surviving rows keep their order and the new rows follow in insertion order. ACCDB and MDB give identical sequences. ID plays no part.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, SQL inserts in the stated order, walk by table-recordset on the named index.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/tie-order-accdb/`, `tie-order-mdb/` (re-read: the walk and SQL sequences, identical in both formats); `evidence/r-sort-3/maintain-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-image-drop-rebuild/`. Discriminators: insertion order scrambled against ID order; index before versus after the rows; delete and reinsert.
- **Open**: whether a reinserted row can take the position of a deleted one when pages are reused (not seen here); SQL's reverse-insertion tie order on a larger table.

### R-SORT-3 Compound framing (ordinary lengths): a compound index orders by its fields in turn, each with its own direction, and the index walk equals SQL ORDER BY modulo ties

- **Claim**: for each of five compound indexes (K,V1), (V1,K), (K desc,V1), (K,V1 desc), (K desc,V1 desc), over keys a, A, b, é and Null and partner values Long, Date, Currency, Double, Yes/No and Byte (including Null, negative, zero and extremes), the forward walk lists the same order as the SQL ORDER BY of the same fields except for ties (same class and same partner value), which the index lists in insertion order (these tables were filled in ID order); the backward walk is the exact reverse; an incrementally built index walks the same as a fresh one. -0 and +0 are different partner keys in the Double table and Seek separates them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, keys at most 20 units; read-only reopen.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/` (re-read: the five walks equal their SQL orders modulo the a/A ties), `mix-order-r/`, `mix-order-d/`, `mix-order-c/`, `mix-order-y/`, `mix-order-b/`, `mix-seek-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-fr-accdb/`, `-mdb/`.
- **Open**: keys near 255 units (next note). Field-framing bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary and Shortened-key collisions: a key whose primary weights reach about 509 bytes loses the order of its tail, and its last unit is ordered by a digest, not by the comparator

- **Claim**: for K = "a" x L in a non-unique index on (K, N), rows inserted N = 2 then N = 1, the walk puts N = 1 first at every L from 100 to 255, in ACCDB and MDB. For é, ß and Han the walk puts N = 1 first up to L = 252 and N = 2 (the earlier inserted row) first from L = 253 (é: also at 255 but not at 254; ß: at 253, 254 and 255; Han: at 253 only, while for 254 and 255 the N order is right and the 255-unit group sorts before the 254-unit group). SQL `ORDER BY K, N` on the same table equals the index walk at every length. A unique index on (K, N) accepts both (K, 1) and (K, 2) at every length and refuses only the true duplicate, and a unique index on K accepts K x (L-1) + 'x' and + 'y' for all four characters, refusing only the exact duplicate (3022); the unique index in the collision probes also kept all five rows. A pair that differs only in its last unit collides when the General primary weights of the key reach about 509 bytes: ß or æ (2 weights each) repeated 254 times + 'x' / 'y' (255 units), and U+FB03 (3 weights) already at 200 units; ß x 253 + 'x' / 'y' (254 units) and every shorter ß length from 200 do not; a decomposed e + U+0301 x 127 (254 units, one primary weight each) does not. For a colliding pair: `ORDER BY K` on the indexed table and on an unindexed copy and the index walk list the 'y' row before the 'x' row (for U+FB03 also the control pair at 200 units), while `A.K < B.K` says x < y (the comparator is right); the equality join `A.K = B.K` returns the pair on the indexed table but not on the unindexed copy (ß, æ and, for U+FB03, the 200-unit pair); and for U+FB03 `WHERE K = (SELECT K ... WHERE ID = 1)` on the indexed table returns no row at all, while the unindexed copy returns the row. Seek `=` finds each of the two rows by its own key (ß, æ, U+FB03, decomposed e); Seek `>` follows the index order. The ß result is the same in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (a, é, ß, Han, æ, U+FB03, e + U+0301) and MDB (a, ß), General dbVersion 128/64; tables LA (non-unique (K,N)), LB (unique (K,N) plus a duplicate), LC and UL (unique K), LD and L (non-unique K, SQL pairs, Seek), L0 (unindexed copy); every refusal read with a marked Errors.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/len-series-a-accdb/`, `len-series-a-mdb-mdb/`, `len-series-e-acute-accdb/`, `len-series-sharp-s-accdb/`, `len-series-han-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q2-fmt-sort/ntail-bracket-e-acute-accdb/`, `ntail-bracket-sharp-s-accdb/`, `ntail-bracket-han-accdb/` (re-read: lengths 248 to 255, walk, backward walk and SQL order), `collide-bracket-sharp-s-accdb/` (re-read: heads 200 to 254, only the 254 pair collides), `collide-expansion-sharp-s/`, `collide-expansion-sharp-s-mdb/`, `collide-expansion-ae-ligature/`, `collide-expansion-ffi-ligature/`, `collide-expansion-decomposed-e/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3/keys-order-index-*/`, `keys-uniqueness-meta-*/`, `keys-seek-*/`. Discriminators: character kind and expansion factor varied with length fixed; length varied from 100 to 255 with the character fixed; indexed table against its unindexed copy; equality, `<`, unique index and Seek on the same pair.
- **Open**: the digest and the retained key bytes, and why é at 254 and Han at 254 and 255 behave differently from 253, need key bytes (T). The earlier statement that the N tail "falls to insertion order" is withdrawn (Corrections 7): a colliding pair is ordered y before x though x was inserted first, so the keys differ and the order is not insertion order.

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary, Memo keys: a Memo index, unique index, GROUP BY and DistinctCount compare only the first 255 units

- **Claim**: a unique index on a Memo column is created on an empty table; two Memo values equal in their first 255 UTF-16 units are one key: m x 255 + 'x' (256 units) collides with + 'y' and with all longer values with the same 255-unit prefix, refused with 3022 (ten rows refused), while m x 254 + 'x' and + 'y' (255 units) and a value changed at the first unit are distinct. The non-unique index on the same 15 rows reads DistinctCount 6, GROUP BY on the Memo column gives the same six classes, and the index walk lists the colliding rows together. A join on a Memo column is refused (0x80020009).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, ASCII 'm' only, lengths 100, 254, 255, 256, 257, 300, 1000.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/memo-prefix-accdb/` (re-read: refusals for IDs 5 to 14, DistinctCount 6 for IX_M and UX_M, GROUP BY classes); `evidence/r-sort-3b/memo-index-ddl/`, `memo-text255-key/`. Discriminators: change at the last unit versus the first, at 254 and 255.
- **Open**: wide characters (255 units versus 255 characters); Memo values in a compound key.

### R-SORT-3 LIKE-RANGE-01/05: a LIKE result set depends on whether an ascending index on the column exists

- **Claim**: on one table, the same LIKE patterns give different row sets with and without an ascending index on K, in ACCDB: with the index, `K LIKE 'a*'` also returns æ (ID 8) and `'e*'` loses the decomposed é (ID 13) while `'é*'` gains it, `'s*'` gains ß, `'f*'` the fi ligature, `'o*'` the œ ligature (the range of a collation-order scan); without it, the pattern is matched unit by unit. A descending-only index changes nothing; dropping the index restores the first results and creating it again restores the second (a repeat); `'a?'` is the same in all states; `K = 'a'` returns IDs 1 and 2 in all states.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, one 30-row table whose only changing factor is the index (none, IX_K, none, IX_KD descending, none, IX_K), the same ten patterns after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/like-index-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-like-vs-range/`, `lu-like-vs-range/`, `lg-ado-like/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/like-prefix-accdb/`. Discriminators: the index added and dropped on one table, descending versus ascending, a repeat of the state.
- **Open**: unique indexes and compound indexes with K first; plan views are T.

## R-CAT-1, R-TXN-1, R-LOCK-1 to R-LOCK-4 and R-CRASH-1

### R-CAT-1 CAT-NEW: the Option, not the name, chooses the format; refusals are fixed
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` gives Version "12.0" for Option 128, as VT_I2, BSTR "128" or VT_R8 128, and when Option is omitted, for `.accdb` and `.mdb` names alike (an `.mdb` name with 128 also gives "12.0"); Option 64 and 192 (64+128) give "4.0" (`Workspace.CreateDatabase` the same); Option 2 (dbEncrypt alone) fails 3001; 32 fails 3170; an existing destination 3204 (file unchanged); a call inside `BeginTrans` 3246 (no file). CollatingOrder is 1033 for the General string used.
- **Conditions**: DAO `DBEngine` and `Workspace.CreateDatabase`, locale `;LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0`, ACE 16.0.20527.20196.
- **Records**: q1/cat-new-argument-forms/ (every form; the .mdb-with-omitted and .mdb-with-128 variants discriminate name from option); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-empty/, create-accdb-option-omitted/, create-accdb-workspace-route/, create-accdb-ext-v40/, create-mdb-empty/, create-mdb-version30/, create-accdb-existing-destination/, create-accdb-in-transaction/.
- **Open**: file sizes differ by version (172,032 B for 12.0, 77,824 B for 4.0, byte classes are W-04); locale strings beyond General, passwords (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-B: name rules in every namespace; TableDefs.Item is the odd lookup
- **Claim**: Table, field, index, relation and query names compare case-insensitively and accent-insensitively: duplicate table 3010, field 3191, index 3375, relation 3012, query 3012 (`Tbl`/`TBL`, `Aa`/`aA`, U+00E9/U+00C9 pairs, `IxA`/`ixa`); a leading space and `. ! [ ]` or a backquote fail 3125; a trailing space is kept; 64 characters are accepted, 65 fail 3001 at `CreateTableDef`, `CreateField` and `CreateRelation` but 3125 for an index name. Precomposed U+00E9 and decomposed `e`+U+0301 are one name for Append, `CREATE TABLE` (3010), `SELECT * FROM [..]` and `DROP TABLE`, but `TableDefs.Item("..")` finds only the spelling stored (3265 for the other), in both creation orders.
- **Conditions**: DAO/SQL on ACCDB, collections refreshed.
- **Records**: q1/cat-b-names-fields-indexes-relations/, q1/cat-b-names-normalization/ (both orders discriminate); evidence/r-cat-1/names/.
- **Open**: MSys rows and IDs (3112, W-04); non-General collations; other accented pairs.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-P: custom properties on every owner, and their rollback
- **Claim**: A custom Long property appended to the Database, a TableDef, a Field, an Index and a QueryDef is read back (Type 4, value) after closing and reopening read-only. Under `BeginTrans`, setting the Database property to 999 and appending a second property are visible in the level; after `Rollback` and `Properties.Refresh` the original value (123456) is back and the new property gives 3270, on the same handle and on a second Workspace. Input grammar: Null/Empty/`""`/empty bytes 3385, missing value 3386, duplicate 3367, type 99 3259, 65-character name 3001, `Type` change 3219, deleting a built-in 3384; Boolean coerces from numbers; types Boolean..GUID round-trip.
- **Records**: q1/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (all owners; Rollback with Refresh); evidence/r-cat-1/properties/, property-inputs/, property-edits/; evidence/r-cat-1b/field-access-property-append/.
- **Open**: LvProp bytes, flag80 (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-Q: saved-query public view and the save-time rendering
- **Claim**: On save, `CreateQueryDef` re-renders SQL: keywords upper-cased, one clause per line with CR LF, a closing `;` plus CR LF, spaces collapsed, identifier case kept (`t.id` stays), `ORDER BY t.id DESC , t.a`, `DELETE` becomes `DELETE *`, `INSERT INTO U ( ID, X )`, a two-predicate `ON T.ID = U.ID AND T.A = U.A` becomes `ON (T.A = U.A) AND (T.ID = U.ID)` (one sample, order reversed), a UNION text is kept as written plus `;`, an embedded tab and a string with `;` survive. The reopened text equals the creating handle's. After reopen `Type` is select 0, union 128, update 48, delete 32, insert 64, crosstab 16 (0 on the creating handle for action queries as before); `ReturnsRecords` -1 for every shape; `Execute` on a select 3065, `OpenRecordset` on an action 3219; getter text set back is stored verbatim until reopen; no U+0000; `PARAMETERS` order fixes Parameters/Fields order; invalid SQL is refused at create (3129/3142/3075).
- **Records**: q1/cat-q-sql-reformat-matrix/ (16 shapes, created and reopened); evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/qd-view-writable-reopen/, qd-create-invalid-sql-errors/; evidence/r-cat-1/querydefs/, sql-round-trip/, order-variants/; evidence/r-cat-1b/qd-execute-on-row-returning/, qd-openrecordset-on-action/.
- **Open**: whether the ON reordering is a rule (one sample); MSysQueries Order bytes (W-04); pass-through execution (ODBC forbidden). Not a universal serializer.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-V: held-object lifetime, parameter state and Requery()
- **Claim**: A held QueryDef whose saved query is deleted answers 3420; recreating the name does not revive it; renaming makes the old name unresolvable (3265); after `Database.Close` held children answer 3420; `Workspace.Close` (also with an open transaction) leaves `Database`, recordset, TableDef, QueryDef and Workspace answering 3420 while `Workspaces.Count` stays 1; `Database.Close` leaves the Workspace usable and its children 3420. `QueryDefs.Item("QP")` twice on one Database returns one shared instance (a value set through one reads through the other); an instance from another Database of the same Workspace or from another Workspace has its own, empty parameter values (open fails 3061 until set). Parameter values persist across executions and after a `SQL` put; a held `Parameters` collection keeps its order after a `SQL` put (reopen reorders). `Recordset.Requery()` with no argument re-runs the open as it was: with a dynaset and a snapshot opened at pV = 20, after pV was set to 40 and after the saved SQL was put to `V < [pV]`, Requery() returned the pV = 20 rows (3, 4, 5) both times; a recordset opened afresh then returned the new definition's rows (1, 2, 3).
- **Records**: q2/cat-v-requery-after-parameter-change/; q1/cat-v-two-instances-parameters/ (four instances), q1/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/ (three Close kinds; resolves evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-workspace-close-held-objects/, whose Database was opened through the default Workspace); evidence/r-cat-1/held-querydef-lifetime/, held-sql-replace/, collection-refresh/.
- **Open**: `Requery(QueryDef)` crashes ACE and is never run.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-D: relationship semantics through DAO
- **Claim**: Each enforced relation adds its own Foreign index on the child, named like the relation; two relations over the same foreign-key column give two indexes (`R1`, `R2`), no sharing, each enforced independently (an insert needs a parent in both; deleting either parent key gives 3200); `Relations.Delete("R1")` removes its index and its enforcement. Also: relation `Attributes` reads back as created; no-enforce adds no index; orphans 3201 (compound keys, self-reference), parent delete/update 3200 unless cascading (also self-reference and a cycle); `Append` over existing orphans 3201 when enforced; type pairs LONG→LONG, COUNTER→LONG, TEXT10→TEXT20 accepted, others 3368; renames propagate; `DROP TABLE` of a parent 3281, `DROP COLUMN` in a relation 3383, DDL under a held recordset 3211 (same process) or 3262 (other process).
- **Records**: q1/cat-d-two-relations-shared-fk-columns/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rel-orphan-insert/, rel-parent-delete-by-attributes/, rel-index-ownership/, rename-field-in-relation/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/cat-drop-table-in-relation/, cat-rel-type-pairs-complete/, rel-orphan-insert-errors/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-rel-self-reference-cascade/, cat-rel-cascade-cycle/, cat-rel-compound-key-enforcement/.
- **Open**: which relation's message an insert violating both names (one sample names the first); MSys/ACL rows, Name AutoCorrect (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-I: saved queries bind by name at each open; rule changes bite at the next Execute
- **Claim**: A saved query holds text only: renames do not rewrite it; the next open fails 3061 (unresolved identifier becomes an implicit parameter) or 3078; dropping a used column succeeds silently and breaks dependents at the next open; the column coming back resolves the parameter; changed nested output changes the outer open; held `Fields`/`Parameters` are snapshots until reopen; wildcard mode follows the client (DAO `*`, ACE ADO `%`). A held saved parameter INSERT and a fresh instance behave the same after each rule change: a field `ValidationRule` gives 3316 with its text, `Required` with Null gives 3314, a unique index with a duplicate gives 3022; a Caption property changes nothing.
- **Records**: q1/cat-i-saved-append-after-rule-change/ (held vs fresh instance, four rule kinds); evidence/r-cat-1/select-star/, rename-column/, drop-column/, unresolved-identifier/, nested-querydef/, recreate-table/, column-type-change/, wildcard-mode/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rename-query-dependent-query/.
- **Open**: function registry, sandbox, cache hit/miss (not observable from DAO).

### R-CAT-1 II-11: CHECK, VIEW, PROCEDURE, OWNERACCESS through the ACE OLE DB provider
- **Claim**: ADO (ANSI-92) accepts `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT .. CHECK (..)` (DAO refuses CHECK with 3289); the rule is enforced for ADO and DAO inserts (error names the rule `CK_V`; DAO 3317), is not checked against existing rows (`CHECK (V < 20)` over a row V = 25 is accepted), and is not visible as DAO `ValidationRule` of the table or field. A column-level `CHECK` in `CREATE TABLE` is refused (3292, no table). `CREATE VIEW` creates a saved select QueryDef (Type 0); `CREATE PROCEDURE` with a select creates a Type 0 query, with a parameter and an UPDATE a Type 48 query with a `PARAMETERS` line; DAO lists them by name; `EXEC P2 1` runs the update; `WITH OWNERACCESS OPTION` survives in the saved SQL of a view; `DROP VIEW`/`DROP PROCEDURE` remove them.
- **Records**: q1/ii11-ado-check-constraint/ (satisfied, violated-by-existing-row, enforced, column-level), q1/ii11-ado-view-procedure-owneraccess/; evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ddl-objects (DAO refusals, per the summary).
- **Open**: SQL CHECK on updates/subqueries, native persistence of the constraint (W-04); secured-workgroup cases are excluded.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-NEST: current-level nesting
- **Claim**: `CommitTrans`/`Rollback` act on the current level; inner commit then outer rollback reverses both; inner rollback then outer commit keeps the outer change; the same row changed at every level takes the outer image after an inner rollback; empty levels raise nothing; resolving with no level raises 3034; the 8th `BeginTrans` raises 3003 (7 levels, also on a second Workspace independently) and writes at levels 6 and 7 raise 3003; `CommitTrans` accepts 0/Missing/Empty/I2 1/UI1 1/R8 1.0/"1", other values 3001 leaving the level open; `BeginTrans(1)` 3001 starts no level; a file opened after a nested begin joins and rolls back with it.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-1/nest-inner-rollback/ (+ -mdb), nest-middle-rollback-after-inner-commit/, nest-outer-rollback-after-inner-commits/, nest-same-row-all-levels/, nest-empty-levels/, nest-resolve-without-level/, nest-late-file-b/; evidence/r-txn-1b/nest-depth-reuse-after-commit/, nest-depth-second-workspace/, nest-depth-recordset-writes/ (+ -mdb), commit-option-values/, commit-option-vartypes/, txn-call-argument-counts/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/nest-depth-recordset-writes-fresh-witness/.
- **Open**: capacity of write levels vs statement failure at level 6.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-STMT: statement scope by failure cause
- **Claim**: Earlier changes always survive and later ones apply. With `dbFailOnError`: duplicate key in `INSERT…SELECT` (first/middle/last/only) 3022, validation 3316, RI delete 3200 and FK 3201 leave no candidate changed (`RecordsAffected` 0); an expression error (error 11) and lock exhaustion (3035) leave the candidates processed before the failure changed; a lock-blocked candidate (another process's pessimistic Edit on its page) gives 3260 after about 2.2 s and the candidates before it in scan order stay changed, those after are untouched (blocked first: nothing changed; middle or last: the earlier ones changed; all: nothing). Without the option: duplicates are skipped silently, a validation-blocked row is skipped, division by zero stores Null, a blocked candidate is skipped silently after the same 2.2 s wait (each blocked page costs one wait: 8.9 s for four) and `RecordsAffected` counts only the updated rows. Same for saved and temporary QueryDef and depth 0-2, on MDB. Through the ACE OLE DB provider (always failing, adExecuteNoRecords) the blocked middle candidate fails `0x80004005 "Could not update; currently locked by user ..."` (ExecuteComplete RecordsAffected 0), rows 1 and 20 before it stay changed and row 64 after it is untouched, in autocommit and inside `BeginTrans`/`CommitTrans`; after the Edit is cancelled the same statement changes all four rows (RecordsAffected 4).
- **Records**: q1/txn-stmt-lock-blocked-positions/ (first, middle, last, all x option/no option); q2/txn-stmt-ado-lock-blocked-byref/; evidence/r-txn-1/stmt-unique-middle-fail-d0/, -d1/, -d2/, -d1-mdb/, stmt-unique-last-fail-d1/, stmt-unique-all-fail-d1/, stmt-unique-middle-plain-d1/, stmt-saved-querydef-fail-d1/, stmt-validation-plain-d1/, stmt-fk-counter-fail-d1/; evidence/r-txn-1b/implicit-*, rb-*; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/stmt-expression-fail-a-survives/.
- **Open**: I/O failure (T); a blocked candidate on the same page as an unblocked one is blocked with it (earlier "whole statement skipped" record, r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-stmt-blocked-no-scope).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-MULTI: files and Workspaces
- **Claim**: Within one Workspace, changes in files A and B resolve together by one `Rollback`/`CommitTrans` in either touch order; a file opened after the first `BeginTrans` joins; a statement failure in B after a change in A is statement-only; closing B inside the level still rolls back both; three handles to one file roll back together. Two Workspaces touching the same two files in opposite order (w1: A then B, w2: B then A) resolve independently: on rows that do not share a page (unindexed field, all four updates admitted) the three resolution orders (w1 Rollback then w2 CommitTrans; w2 CommitTrans then w1 CommitTrans; w2 Rollback then w1 CommitTrans) each leave exactly the committing Workspace's changes on both files and nothing of the rolled-back one (A and B rows 1/64: (0,10), (1,20), (2,20)), in each Workspace's own reads and in a third Workspace after a pause and Idle; no deadlock. An update colliding with the other Workspace's page (three rows on one page) is refused 3188. A refused second-file admission (`OpenDatabase` on a file another process holds exclusively, 3045) leaves the level usable: later updates and `CommitTrans` succeed and both main-file changes are committed (independent final read), and the file opens after the other process closes.
- **Records**: q2/txn-multi-two-workspaces-far-rows-orders/, q1/txn-multi-two-workspaces-opposite-order/, q1/txn-multi-second-file-held-exclusive/; evidence/r-txn-1b/multi-a-then-b-rollback/, multi-b-then-a-rollback/, multi-b-fails-after-a-commit/, multi-close-b-in-txn/, multi-same-file-handles/; evidence/r-txn-1/nest-late-file-b/.
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-LOCAL: lockers inside and across processes
- **Claim**: With a transaction (or pessimistic Edit) on row 1, a write to row 1 or to row 2 (same page) fails after the retry time with 3260 across processes and 3188 across Workspaces of one process (ADO Connections: `0x80004005 "Could not update; currently locked."`), `RecordsAffected` 0, nothing changed; a far row (ID 64) updated through an unindexed field and another table are admitted, and that change survives the first locker's Rollback and CommitTrans; an update of the indexed N on a far row is refused 3188 in-process (an index leaf is a conflict resource); after the holder's resolution the retried write succeeds. In-process (two Workspaces, far rows 1 and 64, unindexed V) all six orders (A:C B:C, A:R B:C, B:C A:C, B:C A:R, A:C B:R, B:R A:C) keep exactly each owner's committed change: final fresh read row 1 V=4 (A committed four times), row 64 V=8 (B committed four times). Two ADO Connections in one process: same row and same page refused, far row (V) and another table admitted, and cn2's committed changes (row 64 V=4, U N=14 after two phases) are kept. The refusal time is `dbLockRetry` x `dbLockDelay` plus 0.01-0.23 s: default 2.01-2.03 s (second card, 3 repeats; 2.21-2.23 s in the first), retry 1 x delay 1 15 ms (3), retry 20 x delay 250 5.08-5.11 s (2), retry 3 x delay 300 0.904 s (3); `dbPageTimeout` alone (100 or 10000) leaves the 2.0 s unchanged (3 each). Three DBEngine objects in one process (CreateObject, CoCreateInstance, ITypeInfo::CreateInstance) are one engine: the same `#Default Workspace#`, an exclusive open beside an exclusive open admitted, `BeginTrans` levels nest across them (three levels), updates of the same row admitted and committed innermost first.
- **Records**: q2/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-unindexed-orders/, q2/txn-local-pagetimeout-alone-timing/ (timestamped brackets), q2/txn-local-two-ado-connections-events/, q2/engine-instances-in-process-admission/, q1/txn-local-conflict-timing/, q1/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-and-orders/ (indexed N refused 7 of 8), q1/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-row/, -same-page/, -far-row/, -other-table/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/.
- **Open**: ADO resolution orders (cn1 never resolved its outer level; ADO's rollback member is `RollbackTrans`, not called); leaf/map byte footprints (W-04); COM threads (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-VIEW: transaction visibility and the refresh boundary
- **Claim**: Uncommitted insert, delete, update and DDL of one Workspace are never visible to another Workspace or process; after a commit another process's held dynaset and table cursors and new snapshots show the value only after a delay: with the default `dbPageTimeout` the first new look is past (4.55, 4.80] s after the update (looks every 250 ms; two earlier samples (4.08, 5.59] and (4.0, 5.5] agree); with `SetOption(dbPageTimeout, 2000)` it was (3.54, 4.04] s and with 1000 (2.07, 4.08] s, so the setting moves the boundary down but not in proportion; the held snapshot stays old until `Requery`, which moves it (and the dynaset) to the new value; `Transactions` is True for dynaset/table, False for snapshot/forward-only; a held dynaset shows a deleted row as a placeholder (3167).
- **Records**: q2/read-refresh-fine-grid/; q1/txn-view-refresh-boundary/, q1/read-refresh-grid/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-view-readers-late/, xproc-uncommitted-membership-late/, xproc-held-cursors-membership-late/, xproc-reader-own-level-late/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ws2-reader-after-other-rollback/, rs-types-after-rollback/.
- **Open**: what fixes the boundary (about 5 s with the default); each look is an observation that may itself refresh.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-CLOSE: what ends and releases a level; Close of a recordset
- **Claim**: `Workspace.Close` with 1, 2 or 3 levels (also after an inner commit, with a pending Edit, with two Databases, or with an update inside the level) returns without error, rolls back everything and leaves every held child 3420; releasing Workspace and Database without `Close` also rolls back; `Database.Close` with the level open keeps the level (Rollback works) and leaves children 3420; a pending Edit/AddNew is dropped by Rollback (`EditMode` 0, `Update` 3020) and survives `CommitTrans`. `Close` of a recordset or clone opened BEFORE `BeginTrans`, called inside the level, raises 3246 in 6 of 7 variants (plain dynaset without any edit, after Edit/Update, original with a clone, clone, clone of an edited original, also on `Workspaces(0)`); the table-type recordset (Seek, Edit/Update) closes without error; the level is not ended: `Rollback` then succeeds in every variant and the row is back at its old value. A recordset opened inside the level closes without error in all eight variants (plain, Edit+Update, clone closed after the original's Update, original with a clone, clone, table-type, Edit+CancelUpdate, AddNew+Update).
- **Records**: q2/txn-close-recordset-opened-before-level/, q1/txn-close-recordset-close-variants/, q1/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/; evidence/r-txn-1b/wsclose-pending-edit-depth-1/, wsclose-two-databases-depth-1/, wsclose-after-inner-commit-depth-2/, wsrelease-no-close-depth-1/; evidence/r-txn-1/workspace-close-depth-1/, workspace-close-depth-3/, close-db-held-recordset/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/wsrelease-no-close-read-first/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/txn-pending-addnew-*/.
- **Open**: what ended the level in evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/clone-close-read-between/ and r-def-cat-txn/txn-close-original-recordset-while-clone-holds-level/ (Rollback then 3034, row at its old value): those programs read the table through a new snapshot inside the level between the Update and the failed Close; the new probe did not. Callbacks (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-COMMIT: an explicit commit is in the file at return
- **Claim**: After the outer `CommitTrans` with the default `dbUserCommitSync` (and with "Yes") the file image is final at the call's return (17.2 MB, then 68.0 MB, unchanged at every look for 500 ms) and a process killed 0 ms after the return leaves the rows visible to the next opener (ACCDB and MDB, 3 of 3); with `SetOption(dbUserCommitSync, "No")` the file has its final size at the return (34.1 MB) but its content still changes at the looks 200 and 250 ms later before it is stable, and the kill 0 ms after the return lost the commit (n=1 per format). An inner forced commit followed by outer Rollback leaves the file unchanged. A small transaction leaves the main file unchanged until the commit; a large one (4,096-row insert) writes pages before the commit (13.1 MB, 28.5 MB, 64.4 MB inside the level) yet a process killed 1.5 s after the Execute, before CommitTrans, leaves the next opener the original 2,048 rows in a 13.0 MB file. In a sampler trace of an explicit transaction (new data pages 49-2188 in the first sample, pages 0, 1, 45-48 and 634 on in the second, none afterwards; one record) the new pages come first. The ETW traces show the mechanism: `CommitTrans` issues the transaction's writes itself, on the calling thread, before it returns; with "No" it writes only the growth bytes and an ACE background thread writes the pages about 508 ms later. Neither issues `FlushFileBuffers`; that comes from the timer of TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND.
- **Conditions**: process-termination model with a live OS cache and public file-image reads; cannot show power-loss durability. The ETW traces see ACE's WriteFile and FlushFileBuffers calls, not the disk.
- **Records**: q2/txn-flush-usercommitsync-no-timeline/ (default, No, Yes), q1/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (txn-inside, txn-committed-0), q1/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/; evidence/r-txn-flush/accdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ and mdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ (3 each), -usercommitsync-no/; evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-txn-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; evidence/r-crash-1-kill/*-txn-committed-ace-first, *-txn-committed-forceflush-ace-first; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-flush-live-images/; evidence/r-sample-rec/pages-large-explicit/.
- **Open**: `dbForceOSFlush` against default: the ETW recorder can now answer it (F).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-OP: an implicit statement is not in the file at return unless ImplicitCommitSync is Yes
- **Claim**: An implicit (non-transactional) `Execute` returns before its change is in the file: killed 0 or 250 ms after return the next opener sees the old count (3 of 3 each, ACCDB and MDB), and the file image read in-process after the return is still the post-statement size at 0 and 250 ms. With `SetOption(dbImplicitCommitSync, "Yes")` the file image is final at the return (17,166,336 B at the first look and unchanged for 500 ms) and a process killed 0 ms after the return leaves the rows present (n=1 per format); the same statement under "No" is not final at the return (29.0 MB at the first look, 34.1 MB at the 200 ms look, a further content change at 250 ms). In the ETW traces the shared-session statement returns with its pages unwritten and an ACE background thread writes them 500 to 545 ms later (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE alike); with "Yes" Execute writes them itself before it returns; in the exclusive session of CreateDatabase every statement writes synchronously.
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/insert-implicit-256/, insert-implicit-4096/, update-in-place-4096/, delete-indexed-4096/, option-implicitcommitsync-yes/; q2/txn-flush-implicitcommitsync-yes-timeline/; evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, *-wait-250ms/, *-implicitcommitsync-yes/; q1/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (t0, t250).
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND: the write-behind window ends about 0.5 s after the return
- **Claim**: After an implicit `Execute` returns, the main file keeps its post-statement image until about 0.5 s later and then gets its final image (and size) in one burst: in three repeats on the big fixture the image was unchanged at 0.43-0.55 s after the return and final by 0.54-0.74 s (signal timestamps); a sampler trace of a 4,096-row implicit INSERT (2,189 pages) showed nothing changed at +0.50 s and all changed pages (0, 1, 46, 49, 50, ...) in the sample at +0.54 s, none later; killed 300 ms (+0.37 s) and 400 ms (+0.48 s) after the return the next opener sees 2,048 rows, at 500 ms (+0.56 s) 4,096, at 1 s 3 of 3, at 250 ms 0 of 3. A small implicit INSERT (6 ms statement) left the file unchanged for the whole 0.47 s sampled and changed it at `Close` (one record). `Database.Close` right after the Execute makes the file final at once (135.8 MB at the first look) and the kill 0 ms after leaves the rows present (n=1 each); `DBEngine.Idle(dbRefreshCache)` and `Idle(dbFreeLocks)` do not (the image is not final at the first look, final at the usual time; kill after Idle(dbRefreshCache): 2,048 rows); opening and closing a snapshot recordset after the statement leaves the file final at the next look (n=1). The same window explains why another process's `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` right after five rapid implicit updates shows the fourth, not the fifth (q1/read-refresh-grid).
- **Records**: q2/txn-flush-boundary-fine-timeline/ (three runs), q2/txn-flush-idle-and-close-timeline/, q2/txn-flush-implicit-kill-after-300ms-return/, -400ms-return/, q2/txn-flush-implicitcommitsync-yes-timeline/ (No run); q1/txn-flush-implicit-kill-after-500ms-return/, -750ms-return/, -idle-refresh-then-kill/, -db-close-then-kill/, -file-image-timeline/; evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, -250ms/, -1000ms/ (3 attempts each), *-wait-3000ms/, *-wait-6000ms/; evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-implicit-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; evidence/r-sample-rec/pages-large-implicit/, insert-implicit/.
- **Open**: what sets the 0.5 s (`dbSharedAsyncDelay` 0 left it unchanged, `dbFlushTransactionTimeout` 1 was mixed; each n=1 to 3); the Idle(dbFreeLocks) and open/close-recordset effects are single samples. The ETW traces name the window: it is the background writer thread's delay (500 to 545 ms), and `Close` writes the pending pages itself on the calling thread.

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-ORDER (issued order) and R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH: ACE flushes once per burst, from a timer thread; Close, CompactDatabase and the exclusive creation session never flush
- **Claim**: In every write probe, ACE issues one `FlushFileBuffers` on the database, from an ACE thread of its own, 2.33 to 2.89 s after the burst of writes, whether a statement, a `CommitTrans` or the background writer issued it, and under `ImplicitCommitSync` Yes and `UserCommitSync` No alike. No call flushes by itself. `Close` writes pending pages, page 1 and a 2-byte field of page 0, and does not flush; a session closed within the delay, the whole exclusive `CreateDatabase` session and `CompactDatabase` (source and new file) are never flushed by ACE. Within a statement the writes are: 2 bytes of page 0 (offset 3584 in the exclusive session, 3586 in shared ones), the pages, the same 2 bytes again. A new page is preceded by a 1-byte write at its last byte.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO, shared and exclusive sessions; ETW Microsoft-Windows-Kernel-File with each operation's process and thread. It shows what ACE asks of the file system. Paging writes (to disk) are attributed only in part, so the disk's order is not shown.
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/ (11 records; `lazy-writer-window` decides that Close does not flush).
- **Open**: what triggers the timer (a period or an idle delay) and its value under continuous load; flushes inside a long transaction; the meaning of the 2-byte field (W-04); torn writes and power loss stay T.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-NATIVE: a read-only session writes nothing to the database
- **Claim**: `OpenDatabase` read-only and shared opens the database with share read/write, creates or opens the lock file and writes its 64-byte record at offset 0, reads the database and writes and flushes nothing in it; `Close` deletes the lock file.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO, one process; ETW trace.
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/readonly-session/.
- **Open**: an MDB, a second reader, and an exclusive read-only open.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-TEMP: lock-count exhaustion only
- **Claim**: With `SetOption(dbMaxLocksPerFile, 5)` a bulk `UPDATE` raises 3035 with `RecordsAffected` 0, earlier changes stay, the level stays usable (`CommitTrans` keeps earlier changes, `Rollback` reverses them, `Database.Close` leaves them uncommitted); implicit statements leave partial progress (COUNT 512, SUM 510) with or without `dbFailOnError`.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-commit/, -rollback/, -close/; evidence/r-txn-1b/implicit-exhaustion-failonerror/, implicit-exhaustion-nofail/.
- **Open**: TEMP/disk faults (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-DDL: catalog changes are transactional
- **Claim**: CREATE/ALTER/DROP TABLE, index add/drop, DAO TableDef append, SELECT INTO, relation drop, QueryDef create/SQL change and database property create are reversed by Rollback (also an outer rollback after inner commit), judged on a fresh Workspace after `Refresh`; a held `TableDef` shows old counts until `Refresh`; DROP under an open recordset 3211; another Workspace or process never lists uncommitted objects; AutoNumber values consumed in the level are not restored.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/ddl-alter-index-drop-rollback-catalog/, ddl-relation-querydef-property-rollback-refresh/, ddl-second-workspace-idle/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ddl-drop-recreate-rollback/, ddl-dao-tabledef-append-rollback/, ddl-inner-commit-outer-rollback/, ddl-held-tabledef-after-rollback/, temp-make-table-rollback/; evidence/r-txn-1/ddl-create-table-rollback/, ddl-nested-failure-outer-rollback/; q1/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (database properties).
- **Open**: counter restoration across a crash.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-SUPPORT: ADO nesting values and events (partial)
- **Claim**: Through the ACE OLE DB provider `Connection.BeginTrans` returns the level (1, 2, 3), `Attributes` reads 0; setting `adXactCommitRetaining` (131072) or `adXactAbortRetaining` (262144) is accepted, and the next `BeginTrans` returns a level one higher after a `CommitTrans` (2 → 3, 4 → 5); `Close` inside a transaction raises 0x800A0CAE; provider properties `Transaction DDL` 16 and `Isolation Levels` 4096; with the outermost level never resolved no change reached the file (independent DAO read: rows unchanged). The recording event sink sees, on the calling thread, WillConnect/ConnectComplete (dispids 6, 7), BeginTransComplete (1) per `BeginTrans`, CommitTransComplete (3) per `CommitTrans`, WillExecute (4) and ExecuteComplete (5) per `Execute` with the command text and the `RecordsAffected` (0 for a refused statement, 1 or 4 for a successful one), Disconnect (8) at `Close`; an `Execute` of an UPDATE returns a closed recordset (`Close` on it 0x800A0E78).
- **Records**: q2/txn-support-ado-rollback-control-events/, q2/txn-local-two-ado-connections-events/ (two Connections), q1/txn-support-ado-nesting/; evidence/r-ops/ado-connection-events/.
- **Open**: rollback through ADO: our probes called `Rollback`, which is not an ADO member (ADO ran it as a command named "Rollback" and refused it, 0x800A0BB9, every time); `RollbackTrans` was never called. ISAM/ODBC participants (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-OPEN: share admission matrix
- **Claim**: Across processes, DAO exclusive read/write refuses every second mode (3045); exclusive read-only admits exclusive/shared read-only and refuses read/write; shared read-only admits shared read-only/read-write and exclusive read-only, refuses exclusive read/write (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); shared read/write admits shared modes and refuses exclusive; native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` opens beside DAO read-only modes and is refused (Win32 32) beside DAO read/write; native read/write share-zero is refused beside every DAO mode and refuses every DAO mode (3045) in both orders; same pattern in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-*-then-dao-* (32 pairs), *-native-r-shareread-then-dao-*, *-native-rw-share0-then-dao-*, *-dao-*-then-native-*.
- **Open**: the 3704/3045 split; actual access/share flags (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-DEFAULT: omitted arguments are shared read/write
- **Claim**: `OpenDatabase(path)` with every optional argument omitted, in two processes, opens in both and reads in both (2,048 rows); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B; after one closes, a default open by the other still works. Same as the explicit shared read/write pair.
- **Records**: q1/ex-default-omitted-arguments/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-rw-then-dao-shared-rw.
- **Open**: an exclusive-only open beside a default open across processes (the probe's attempt was admitted in-process after b's own open; EX-PUBLIC rules).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-CREATE: creation beside other processes
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` of a path that exists is refused 3204 and does not touch the file, whether the other process created it a moment before (a started 0.25 s earlier, b called during that window), holds it open (exclusive: `OpenDatabase` by the other process 3045) or has closed it; the winning creator's file opens (Version 12.0, 172,032 B) in an independent final read. While the first creator is frozen inside `CreateDatabase` (100 and 200 ms after it started) no file exists (opens 3024), a second creator of the same path succeeds and holds it exclusively (a native read open Win32 32), and the frozen creator, once resumed, gets 3204; the finished file opens with its five system tables.
- **Records**: q2/crash-new-suspend-mid-create-100ms/, -200ms/; q1/ex-create-race-two-creators/ (one overlap), q1/ex-create-over-held-destination/ (held, then closed); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-existing-destination/.
- **Open**: cleanup with pending I/O (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-ALIAS: path spellings across processes; the extended spelling is refused
- **Claim**: With A holding the plain path exclusive, B opening the upper-case, `.\` or slash spelling shared read/write is refused 3045 as for the plain path; unheld, those spellings open.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-alias-xproc-upper/, -dot/, -slashes/; q1/ex-alias-extended-path-unheld-control/ (plain, slashes, dot, upper open unheld; the absolute `\\?\C:\...` spelling gives 3043 with nobody holding the file, so ACE refuses that spelling itself, as in evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-alias-*-extended/).
- **Open**: hard links and 8.3 names (T); UNC extended forms.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PROBE: metadata probing beside every DAO mode
- **Claim**: A `FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` open with all sharing succeeds beside every DAO mode (also exclusive read/write); a data read through it gives Win32 5; the lock file opens for read with all sharing when present (64 B shared, absent under exclusive opens, Win32 2).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-dao-excl-rw-then-metadata-probe/, mdb-dao-shared-ro-then-metadata-probe/ (representative of 16).
- **Open**: `FileIdInfo` itself is not requested by the driver.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-RACE: an opener frozen before it has the file reserves nothing
- **Claim**: Process a calls `OpenDatabase` exclusive and is frozen 5 ms or 25 ms later (its call not returned): process b opens the same file shared and then exclusive, both succeed, a native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` open fails Win32 32 (b holds it exclusive); after b closes and a is resumed, a's open returns a Database and reads 2,048 rows, and a final open by b succeeds. Nothing of the half-finished open was visible to b.
- **Conditions**: whole-process suspension (SuspendParticipant), local NTFS; where inside the call a stopped is not visible.
- **Records**: q2/ex-race-suspend-opener-mid-open-5ms/, q2/ex-race-suspend-opener-mid-open-25ms/.
- **Open**: path replacement and injected failure after identity probing (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PUBLIC and R-LOCK-2 RO-INPROC: in-process admission
- **Claim**: In one process (same or second Workspace) a second binding is admitted more widely than across processes: beside an exclusive or shared read/write first binding all four modes open; beside exclusive read-only, exclusive and shared read/write are refused (3045); beside shared read-only, exclusive read/write is refused (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); reads succeed in both, also after the first closes. Several DBEngine objects of one process are one engine (see TXN-LOCAL), so their opens follow the same in-process rules.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-inproc-ws1-*-then-*, accdb-inproc-ws2-*-then-*; q2/engine-instances-in-process-admission/.
- **Open**: alias spellings in-process do not discriminate.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PERM and R-LOCK-2 RO-PERM: file attributes
- **Claim**: With the database read-only, shared and exclusive read/write opens fail 3051, read-only opens succeed (`Updatable` False; lock file 64 B for shared, none for exclusive); with a read-only lock file, shared modes fail (read/write 3051, read-only 3050) and exclusive modes open; with both the database and a 64-byte lock file read-only, shared read-only 3050 and read/write 3051, exclusive read/write 3051 and exclusive read-only opens (count read, `Updatable` False) with the lock file untouched (hash f5a5fd42d16a unchanged); a stale writable lock file stops no mode and is replaced.
- **Records**: q1/ex-perm-readonly-db-and-lockfile-exclusive/ (completes the matrix); evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-db-readonly-all-modes/, accdb-readonly-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-stale-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-db-and-lockfile-readonly/ and mdb counterparts.
- **Open**: folder ACLs (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-LIFE: what keeps an exclusive file held
- **Claim**: After `Recordset.Close`+`Database.Close` or release without `Close`, a second process opens read/write within about 25 ms. With a table recordset and a TableDef held: releasing only the Database keeps the exclusion (3045); closing the recordset (TableDef still held) ends it; releasing the TableDef changes nothing more. With an open transaction on a created Workspace, the exclusion stays after the Database and recordset are released (3045), ends at `Rollback`, and the rolled-back row reads unchanged in an independent open. A killed exclusive holder leaves no lock file and the next opener opens; killed 40 ms into an implicit `INSERT…SELECT` (its call not returned) the file is available to a shared open and to a native read open 0.1 s later, still at 0, 100, 300 and 1000 ms, and the file is unchanged (8,663,040 B, 2,048 rows).
- **Records**: q2/ex-life-kill-mid-statement-exclusive/; q1/ex-life-child-and-transaction-owner/ (seven attempts after seven states); evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-life-close/, accdb-life-release/ (+ mdb); evidence/r-crash-1-kill/accdb-kill-open-only-exclusive-ace-first/, mdb-...
- **Open**: pending I/O itself, inherited handles (T); each state sampled once.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-NATIVE and RO-PAIR (lock-file slots): a shared session holds one lock byte per slot and writes its record at the slot

- **Claim.** A DAO shared read-only session holds a one-byte lock on the lock file at 0x10000000 + n for its slot n, counted from 1, and its 64-byte record is at (n − 1) × 64: the computer name and `admin`, each NUL-terminated and space-padded to 32 bytes. A second session in another process takes slot 2 (lock 0x10000002, record at 64; the file grows to 128 B). When the second closes, its lock byte is released and its record stays; the last close deletes the file. The database file carries no byte-range lock at all. An open table-type recordset adds two lock bytes on the lock file, 0x40005401 and 0x50005401.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; read-only shared opens in one and in two processes; locks found by bisecting [0, 2^62) with exclusive fail-immediately LockFileEx through a separate handle.
- **Records.** w-10-readers-only/lock-ranges-shared-readonly, lock-ranges-two-readers; r-etw-rec/readonly-session (the record write at offset 0, deletion at Close).
- **Open.** The encoding of the recordset's lock bytes (one observation: 0x54 and slot 1 are candidates); MDB `.ldb`; slot reuse after a departed session in a third open; whether the slot lock is exclusive or shared.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-PAIR: readers coexist
- **Claim**: Two and three shared read-only readers coexist and read (2,048 rows each); exclusive read-only is admitted beside a shared read-only reader (against the book's expectation); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B and is gone when all close.
- **Records**: q1/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/ (a, b, c); evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-shared-ro, *-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-excl-ro; evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-readonly-db-two-readers/, mdb-...
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-ACTUAL: DAO read-only opens hold no database write access
- **Claim**: A DAO shared or exclusive read-only open does not hold write access to the database file: a native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` open succeeds and reads 4096 bytes beside it, and fails Win32 32 beside DAO read/write; read-only readers permit writers (share-write).
- **Conditions**: inferred from Windows share semantics and refusals, not from a handle trace.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-shared-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-shared-rw-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-rw-then-native-r-shareread/.
- **Open**: direct handle observation (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-2 RO-LATE: writers beside readers
- **Claim**: With DAO shared read-only readers, a DAO shared read/write writer opens and updates (`RecordsAffected` 1) while two or three readers are open, after one closes and after all close; the readers then (7 s, Idle) read the writer's value from a new snapshot and see an intact file; a DAO exclusive writer and a native read/write share-zero writer are refused. With native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` readers a and b, the DAO shared read/write open and the DAO exclusive open are refused 3045 and the native share-zero open Win32 32 with both held and with b alone held; after both close the writer opens and updates, native share-zero opens, and the update reads back.
- **Records**: q1/ro-late-native-lease-then-dao-writer/, q1/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-ro-late/, mdb-ro-late/.
- **Open**: none that blocks (the exclusive-open attempts after the process's own open were admitted in-process and are not evidence).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ROLE: a pending Edit does not block readers
- **Claim**: While another process holds a pessimistic `Edit` on row 1, readers (snapshot, dynaset, forward-only, table; held and newly opened) read row 1 and the unedited values without error.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-role-edit-existing-readers/, read-role-edit-then-update-new-reader/, write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/.
- **Open**: native lock purpose (T).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-PATH: a reader beside a writer stopped inside a statement (partial)
- **Claim**: With a writer process frozen (all threads suspended) 60 ms into `INSERT INTO T SELECT ...` of 4,096 rows with 2,000-character memos (its call not returned; the file at 20.1 MB, the lock file 128 B once the reader is in), another process opens read-only without error or wait and reads only the old committed state: COUNT 4,096, SUM/MIN/MAX of N unchanged, memo lengths 2,000, an `IX_N` table cursor (first row, `Seek >= 20000` finds 2000/20000, last row 4096); after the writer is resumed and a pause, a fresh read gives 8,192 rows and the new SUM. The same holds in r-ops (COUNT 0 on the table while the writer is frozen mid-INSERT).
- **Records**: q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-insert/; evidence/r-ops/suspend-writer-mid-insert/.
- **Open** (all single samples): a writer frozen 40 ms into `UPDATE T SET M = String(3500,'y')`: the reader's aggregate over `LEN(M)` failed 3624 "Could not read the record; currently locked by another user", other reads old and intact (q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-memo-replace); a reader frozen mid-scan while another process updated all 4,096 N: the writer was admitted without waiting and the resumed reader returned the new SUM(N) (q2/read-path-reader-frozen-while-writer-updates, its position in the scan unknown); an index-relocating `UPDATE T SET N = N + 1000000` had already returned when the freeze came (q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-indexed-update); native acquisitions and page-level coherence (T).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-REFRESH: the staleness boundary and Idle
- **Claim**: Another process's committed or implicit update becomes visible to a held dynaset, a held table cursor and a new snapshot between 4.55 and 4.80 s after the update with the default `dbPageTimeout` (looks every 250 ms; the earlier samples (4.0, 5.5] and (4.08, 5.59] agree), not to a held snapshot until `Requery`; with `dbPageTimeout` 2000 between 3.54 and 4.04 s; `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` before that time changes nothing. After five rapid implicit updates (+1 each, 100 ms apart), `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` 15 ms after the last shows the fourth value (1014), not the fifth (1015); after 7 s it shows 1015: Idle shows the last flushed state, so the earlier 1001-versus-1002 ladder is the write-behind window (TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND).
- **Records**: q2/read-refresh-fine-grid/ (250 ms looks, then PageTimeout 2000); q1/read-refresh-grid/ (0.25-7 s grid, then the five-update ladder), q1/txn-view-refresh-boundary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-refresh-elapsed/, read-refresh-ladder/.
- **Open**: what fixes the boundary (see TXN-VIEW).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ID: a deleted-and-reinserted row
- **Claim**: After another process deletes row 5 and reinserts the same key and values (twice, the second time with changed values), a held dynaset positioned on it, read after 7 s and Idle, answers 3167 "Record is deleted" for every field at that position (its Bookmark bytes unchanged, 01070000) and neighbours read normally; `Requery` shows the reinserted row (N=51, V=9) at the same place with different bookmark bytes (01030000); a new snapshot shows it.
- **Records**: q1/read-id-delete-reinsert-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-id-delete-reinsert/ (stale, superseded).
- **Open**: a bookmark can differ because Requery rebuilds the keyset, so bookmark equality is not an identity test.

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SCHEMA: held descriptors across another process's DDL
- **Claim**: When the other process holds no cursor on the table, another process's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `CREATE TABLE` all succeed; the first process's held TableDef still answers (Name, Fields.Count 3 and Indexes.Count 1) after 7 s and Idle, with no error; a refreshed `TableDefs` Item shows the new 4 fields; the held Fields collection shows 4 after its own `Refresh`; a new `SELECT *` reads the recreated table. A held QueryDef is a snapshot of its definition as of the last collection refresh: after another process set the saved SQL (7 s earlier, Idle) the held `qt.SQL` still read the old text and a fresh `Item` from a refreshed collection read the new one; after that process deleted and recreated `QT` with other SQL, the held object still answered (Name "QT", no 3420) with the previous text while a fresh `Item` read the newest and `SELECT * FROM QT` ran the newest; the held QueryDef still opened a recordset.
- **Records**: q2/read-schema-held-querydef-late/; q1/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-schema-held-descriptors/ (3262 with held cursors).
- **Open**: what the held QueryDef's recordset returned (not read).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SNAPSHOT: unread rows
- **Claim**: A snapshot opened before another process updates rows 20 and 40 shows their open-time values when first read after 7 s and Idle (N=200, 400, old S) and the new values only after `Requery`; a dynaset opened at the same time shows the new values (7777, 8888, 'changed') at first read of those rows; its first row, read before the update, was not re-read.
- **Records**: q1/read-snapshot-unread-rows/ (three rows on separate pages).
- **Open**: lazy materialization of large snapshots; callbacks (T).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TIMING: edit ownership vs writers
