- **Claim**: A pending pessimistic `Edit` of an unindexed field (V) of row 1 blocks another process's update of an unindexed field (3260) and of an indexed field (N) of row 2 (same page), with the ADO locking mode first-opened as page (0) and as row (1, error 3218 "currently locked"); it admits V of row 64 and an INSERT; after `CancelUpdate` the row 2 update succeeds. Also: optimistic/`dbSeeChanges` admits all; the block lasts only while `Edit` is pending (admitted after CancelUpdate, Update, recordset Close or release, `Database.Close`); `dbDenyWrite` gives 3262 for UPDATE/table open and `dbDenyRead` 3261.
- **Records**: q1/write-timing-unindexed-field-page-mode/, -row-mode/ (field and mode variants); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/, write-timing-optimistic-seechanges/, write-timing-release-boundaries/, write-timing-ado-lock-mode-page/, -row/, write-deny-options/.
- **Open**: the release probe never showed the Edit still pending at each step.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ORIGINAL: first Update conflicts, second Update overwrites
- **Claim**: With an optimistic dynaset edit (N = 100) pending, another process changing the same row (N), only another field (V), changes the row so that the first `Update` fails 3197 (`EditMode` stays 1); a second `Update` succeeds (`EditMode` 0) and overwrites the whole row, so the other field's change is lost (final independent read: N=100, V=0); the other process changing N and restoring the original value gives no conflict (first Update succeeds; a second Update gives 3020). Final values read by the other process after a 7 s pause and Idle.
- **Records**: q1/write-original-matrix-final-read/ (three rows on separate pages); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-original-same-row/, write-original-same-row-mdb/, write-original-changed-then-restored/.
- **Open**: a change to another row on the same page.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ALLOC: an uncommitted insert blocks the page it lands on
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert of key 500 into T, an insert of key 501 into T (3260) and an update of row 64 (the last page) (3260) are refused, an insert into another table, an insert into the AutoNumber table and an update of row 1 (first page) are admitted; after the rollback the T insert succeeds.
- **Records**: q1/write-alloc-insert-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-alloc-insert-txn/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-alloc-counter-rollback-readid/.
- **Open**: byte-level page identity (W-04); an earlier insert was admitted while all rows of the table were updated (write-promote), which depends on free space.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TREE: concurrent uniqueness candidates
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert, inserts of the same primary key, other keys and the same secondary-unique value fail 3260; after its rollback they insert; after its commit the same key or value gives 3022. With WRITE-ALLOC the block is the page the new row lands on.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-tree-unique-secondary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-tree-unique-candidate/, write-alloc-insert-txn/.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-LV: memo replacement is blocked by a recordset that has read the memo
- **Claim**: With another process's dynaset positioned on row 4 of a table of 3,000-character memos and having READ that row's memo `Value`, replacing the memo of row 4 (3,500+ characters, `dbFailOnError`) fails 3186 "Could not save; currently locked by user ..." twice, while rows 3 and 1 are replaced; once the recordset is closed, row 4 is replaced and all lengths read back. A dynaset merely positioned on row 1 (no Edit, no memo read) did not block replacing rows 1 to 4; with `Edit` pending on row 1, replacing rows 1, 2 and 3 fails 3260 (page granularity); after `CancelUpdate` and Close the replacement succeeds (lengths 3,501, 3,500 x3).
- **Records**: q2/write-lv-replace-after-memo-read/ (3186 twice); q1/write-lv-replace-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-lv-replace-reuse/ (the earlier 3186).
- **Open**: whether a memo read in the same page blocks neighbours (only the read row was blocked).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-DDL: schema changes beside other users
- **Claim**: Another process's `ALTER TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `DROP INDEX` fail 3262 while a process has cursors open, a pending pessimistic Edit (also after CancelUpdate with the recordset open) or an uncommitted DDL; its `SELECT` of a table under uncommitted DDL gives 3261; all succeed after the other side closes or rolls back, and also while the other process holds only descriptors (TableDef, QueryDef).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-ddl-vs-pending-edit/, read-schema-held-descriptors/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-ddl-vs-open-transaction-ddl-first/, cat-xproc-drop-table-under-reader/; q1/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/, q2/read-schema-held-querydef-late/.

### R-LOCK-4 TXN-RANGE: an unrelated admitted change survives the other's resolution
- **Claim**: While process a holds a transaction updating N of row 1, process b updates V of row 64 (far) and U row 1 (admitted) and V of row 2 (same page, 3260); after a's Rollback (phase 1) b reads row 1 N=10, row 64 V=1, U N=11 and a reads the same; after a's CommitTrans (phase 2) both read row 1 N=1010, row 64 V=2, U N=12 (b after 7 s and Idle; a after 7 s and Idle).
- **Records**: q1/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/ (in-process); q2/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-unindexed-orders/.
- **Open**: lock release at an inner level.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-PROMOTE: lock budget
- **Claim**: Exceeding `dbMaxLocksPerFile` raises 3035 (statement fails, `RecordsAffected` 0, transaction usable, Rollback works, other process admitted); no promotion is observed: another table and an INSERT stay admitted while a process holds 64 row updates.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-promote-bulk-update/; evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-*/.
- **Open**: the default threshold (no getter).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-FAIL: a stopped or killed holder
- **Claim**: With another process holding an uncommitted update of row 1 and a pessimistic Edit on row 40 (first card), or an open transaction after `UPDATE T SET S = 'held'` over all 4,096 rows (second card, the process frozen), writes to its rows fail 3260 (after the full retry wait, frozen or running); 1.5 s after the holder is killed (TerminateProcess) the same writes succeed, its uncommitted changes are gone (row 1 N=10 then +1 by the survivor, S 'row', independent read after 7 s and Idle) and the lock file stays at 128 B.
- **Records**: q2/write-fail-suspend-holder-mid-update/; q1/write-fail-kill-lock-holder/.
- **Open**: the release time inside 1.5 s; a freeze inside the acquiring statement (the update had returned before the freeze); injected failures (T).

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-NEW: the new file appears all at once
- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CreateDatabase` of a new path (about 250 ms) leaves no file at 5, 40, 150 and 200 ms after it started (killed; opens 3024) and a complete file at 250 and 300 ms (172,032 B, Version 12.0, the five system tables, opened by the next opener; hashes differ between runs); with the creator frozen at 100 and 200 ms no file exists on disk either. A partial or zero-length file was never seen; after a kill before the file appears a later `CreateDatabase` of the path succeeds, after one that left a complete file it gives 3204.
- **Conditions**: process termination or suspension of an owned process, OS and cache alive; kills at the stated delays after the start signal, one attempt each.
- **Records**: q2/crash-new-kill-mid-create-200ms/, -250ms/, -300ms/, q2/crash-new-suspend-mid-create-100ms/, -200ms/; q1/crash-new-kill-mid-create-5ms/, -40ms/, -150ms/.
- **Open**: states between 200 and 250 ms; cut reconstruction of the initial-file writes (T).

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-KILL: process-termination images open cleanly (serves CRASH-CAPTURE)
- **Claim**: In the live-OS process-termination model, each of 48 frozen images (34 kill points on ACCDB and MDB, 12 mid-call kills, one uncommitted bulk transaction, and a freeze-then-kill 60 ms into a 4,096-row insert, one after a statement that had returned, one after a commit that had returned) opens ACE-first with `hr=0`, no error, no repair request and an unchanged file; Compact-first also succeeds (17.2 MB, 4,096 rows); uncommitted transactions (also a 2,048-row insert in a transaction killed 1.5 s after Execute: 13.0 MB file, 2,048 rows visible), pending AddNew, edited dynaset rows, implicit statements (also a frozen 4,096-row insert at 21.2 MB: 4,096 original rows, SUM(N) 83,906,560, memo lengths 2,000, IX_N cursor first/last row intact), LV replace and bulk insert are absent; committed (default and forced) and closed states are present (a 4,096-row insert committed before the freeze: 8,192 rows, SUM(N) 577,413,120); the lock file stays 64 B after a shared writer is killed and is absent after an exclusive writer.
- **Conditions**: `TerminateProcess` (also after a full-process suspension), OS and cache alive, kill at API boundaries or 20/60/100/250/400/1500 ms into a call; not power loss, no write recording.
- **Records**: q2/crash-control-suspend-then-kill-mid-insert-early/, -late/, crash-control-suspend-mid-commit-then-kill/; q1/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/; evidence/r-crash-1-kill/* (32 kill records), evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/* (12).
- **Open**: a freeze inside `CommitTrans` (the call had returned before the 8 ms freeze), Access-first, CalcDb cuts, power loss (T).

## R-CURSOR-1, R-DAO-1 and R-ERR-1

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POS: position, BOF/EOF and Move transitions are one rule set across table, dynaset and snapshot; forward-only is a restricted subset

- **Claim.** On table-type, dynaset and snapshot: `MovePrevious` on the first row gives BOF without error, a second gives 3021; `MoveNext` past the last row gives EOF, a second gives 3021; `Move 0` at BOF/EOF gives 3021; `Move n` and `Move -n` past either end give EOF/BOF without error; `Move -1` from EOF and `Move 1` from BOF reach the last/first row. `AbsolutePosition` is -1 at BOF/EOF and 0-based otherwise (table-type: 3251). An empty table has BOF and EOF true and every move gives 3021. Deleting the current row through the recordset itself leaves BOF/EOF false and the next field read fails 3021 on a dynaset (3167 on table-type); a row deleted by another cursor reads 3167 on a dynaset. A failed `FindNext`/`FindPrevious` sets NoMatch and leaves the position; a later `Move` resets NoMatch. Forward-only: `MovePrevious`, `MoveFirst`, `MoveLast`, negative `Move` and `AbsolutePosition` fail 3219; `Move 0` and positive moves work. `Move` takes fractional and digit-string counts rounded to a long (2.5 and 1.5 both move 2), refuses Null and text with 3421, and accepts the 32-bit limits; `Move(Rows:=2)` named binds as dispid 0 (q2/named-dao-order-variants).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, all four types; the table-type and dynaset/snapshot move set also on an MDB (Jet 4). Find family on dynaset and snapshot only (table-type 3251, forward-only 3219).
- **Records.** c1/pos-moves-dynaset, pos-moves-snapshot, pos-moves-table, pos-moves-forwardonly, pos-empty-singleton, pos-delete-ends, pos-find; c1b/move-argument-forms, find-duplicates-sequence, find-all-directions-other-types, find-empty-and-ends; def/mdb-pos-table, mdb-pos-dynaset, mdb-pos-snapshot, delete-by-other-cursor-current-row; fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row. Discriminating: delete by the cursor itself (3021) versus by another cursor (3167): c1/pos-delete-ends against def/delete-by-other-cursor-current-row and fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row; q/id-held-field-after-delete-reinsert and q2/bulk-getrows-cursor-on-deleted-row repeat the 3167 reading.
- **Open.** `Move(2147483647)` on forward-only gives 3219 while `Move 10` works; forward-only `RecordCount` is -1 on a singleton but grows on a larger table; Find with quote and Like criteria differs on an indexed Text column (fix/find-text-quotes-unindexed, find-like-patterns-unindexed). Lanes other than raw IDispatch.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EMPTY-PAGES (public part): a table whose rows are all deleted, and a table with deleted ranges, behave as empty or as the surviving rows on every type

- **Claim.** A table whose 256 rows were all deleted reports BOF and EOF true, `RecordCount` 0 and every move 3021 on table, table with `Index`, dynaset, snapshot and forward-only (forward-only 3219 for `MoveFirst`, `MoveLast`, `MovePrevious`). A table with deleted id ranges returns the survivors in storage order (table-type without Index) or key order (with Index, and dynasets over the table or an ORDER BY), both directions, EOF true at the end of the forward walk and BOF at the end of the backward walk; table-type `RecordCount` is 228 at open. A snapshot over the plain table name or an unordered `SELECT` also walks in storage order (q2: `Move 998` from position 1 lands on ID 982 of a table whose rows were inserted by doubling).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; fixtures MA (256 rows deleted) and M2 (shuffled storage order, 228 survivors). Whether any page is actually empty is not established.
- **Records.** c1/pages-all-deleted, pages-traverse-table, pages-traverse-dynaset; fix/create-pages, pages-traverse-storage-order (discriminating: storage and key order differ); q2/pop-snapshot-plain-table-big.
- **Open.** (T) Page-level emptiness, overflow-only pages, budget and I/O failures: need W-04 byte interpretation and fault injection; the sampling recorder (r-sample-rec) records page hashes of INSERTs only.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POP: RecordCount, AbsolutePosition and PercentPosition; the 500-row prefetch frontier; only an ORDER BY snapshot is complete at open

- **Claim.** Table-type reports the exact `RecordCount` at open. A dynaset and a snapshot over a plain table or an unordered `SELECT` report 1 at open and after a field read; the first `MoveNext` populates up to 500 further rows (228-row table: the full 228; 2048-row sources: 501); moves inside that range leave the count (positions 10, 100, 500: 501); a jump beyond it populates exactly to the row reached (position 2000: 2001); `MoveLast` gives 2048, and `MoveLast` alone on a new recordset also gives 2048; the dynaset over `SELECT ... ORDER BY` behaves the same. A snapshot over `SELECT ... ORDER BY` reports the full count at open (2048 rows and 8 rows) and its values are fixed at open: an UPDATE issued after open, after the first move, or after `MoveLast`+`MoveFirst` is never seen, on either size. A snapshot over a plain table or an unordered `SELECT` takes a row's values when the row is first reached: rows reached after the update read the new value (position 1 and position 2000 read 'chg'), a row already read stays stale (c1/pop-visibility-snapshot). `Requery` returns a dynaset to 1. Query sources: DISTINCT, TOP, crosstab give 1 at open and the exact count after `MoveLast`; GROUP BY, UNION, LEFT JOIN give the exact count at open. Forward-only counts the rows fetched. `AbsolutePosition` is `RecordCount`-1 at the last row; `PercentPosition` has its own note below.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; MDB rows for the 8-row table and query sources; sizes 8, 228, 512 and 2048 rows.
- **Records.** q2/pop-snapshot-plain-table-big, pop-snapshot-select-noorder-big, pop-snapshot-orderby-small (discriminating: ORDER BY on 8 rows is complete at open, plain and unordered on 2048 rows are not); q/pop-frontier-dynaset-big (minimal observation `MoveLast` alone equals the instrumented), pop-frontier-snapshot-big, pop-snapshot-change-after-open, pop-snapshot-change-after-first-move, pop-snapshot-change-after-movelast; c1/pop-count-dynaset, pop-count-snapshot, pop-count-table, pop-count-forwardonly, pop-count-minimal-dynaset, pop-visibility-snapshot; def/mdb-pos-dynaset, mdb-pos-snapshot, pos-query-*; fix/pages-traverse-storage-order; c1b/percent-assign.
- **Open (F).** Whether the unvisited rows inside the first 500-row chunk take their values at the first `MoveNext` or at their visit (both records changed the rows before the first move): one snapshot with a change between a `MoveNext` and a read of a row inside the chunk. (T) Other Workspace/process controls (R-LOCK-3/4).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POP (PercentPosition): a scale per recordset type; dynaset and indexed assignment by truncated ordinal; table-type storage-order assignment open

- **Claim.** Dynaset and snapshot: `PercentPosition` reads 100 × `AbsolutePosition` / `RecordCount` as a Single, over the rows populated so far (0.1996008 at the first of 501 rows), and fails 3021 without a current row (at EOF, on an empty recordset). Assignment converts the value to Single (a digit string converts, Empty is 0, Null gives 3421) and takes the ordinal trunc(p × `RecordCount` / 100) of the populated rows; it never populates further. An ordinal equal to the count gives the last populated row; one outside 0..count gives 3001 and keeps the position (count 1: 199 lands, 200 refuses; count 20: 104.9 and −4.9 land, 105 and −5 refuse). Forward-only gives 3219. Table-type: 0 at BOF; at EOF 100 in storage order, also on an empty table, where assignment gives 3001, and 0 under an index (def/mdb-pos-table, a single observation on an MDB). In storage order the getter is 100 × (k + r / l) / N: N is the number of data pages in the table's usage map, pages with no live row included; k is the ordinal of the row's page among them; r is the row's rank among the page's l live rows (M: 3.5714285 at the first row, 99.55357 at the last; H, half-live pages: 0.6896552 at ID 3). Under an index, assignment takes the ordinal trunc(p × `RecordCount` / 100) in index order (U: 50 gives ID 129; G: 57.5 gives ID 182, past the deleted range), and the getter reads 100 × (ordinal − 1) / `RecordCount`, 0 on the first row, at every row of U.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; tables of 20, 175, 228 and 256 rows on 1 to 29 pages.
- **Records.** pct/getter-walk-u, getter-walk-h, getter-walk-g, index-u, index-g; c1b/percent-table-pages, percent-assign, percent-assign-bounds, percent-partial-population, position-assign-empty-single; dao-1b/recordset-members-at-eof, recordset-members-forward-only; c1/pop-count-forwardonly; def/mdb-pos-table. Discriminating: getter-walk-h against getter-walk-u (live rows count, not slots: half-live pages read in steps of 1/5 and 1/4 of a page); getter-walk-g (a page whose rows are all deleted stays in the map and counts; freed pages do not).
- **Open.** (1) Storage-order assignment on more than one page. The landing is a function of the value and the table alone: five start rows (pct/assign-starts-u) and ascending and descending sequences (assign-sequential-u) give the rows of fresh recordsets (assign-fresh-u). It is not the getter's inverse and not monotone in the value (U: 36.5 gives ID 90, 37 gives ID 82; M: 7.1 gives ID 102). Its page is about floor(p × N / 100) − 1, and its slot advances with a slope that grows with the page and wraps within the page; the rule is not identified. CalcDb takes the getter's inverse, which is ACE's answer on one page, and the questions count the difference. (2) The table-type refusal bounds on more than one page: M refuses −1 and 103.6 and accepts 101; B (one page) refuses beyond one row on either side. (3) The index getter reads the ordinal itself, not ordinal − 1, at every fifth row of G (175 rows); ACE's estimate there is not identified.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-ID: Bookmark representation, assignment errors, validity after Requery, and the held Field across delete and reinsert

- **Claim.** `Bookmark` is `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1`, 4 bytes, lower bound 0; forward-only is not bookmarkable (3219). Dynaset: a 16-bit little-endian number in bytes 0-1 (row 1 = 0x0200, +1 per row for the first ~400 rows: row 101 = 0x0264, 401 = 0x0390); it is not ordinal + 0x200 beyond that: an extra 59 appears four times, between rows 401 and 501 (row 501 = 0x042f), 901 and 1001, 1301 and 1401, 1801 and 1901 (row 1001 = 0x065e, 1401 = 0x0829, 1901 = 0x0a58, last row 2048 = 0x0aeb); bytes 2-3 are 0. Snapshot: the same shape with base 0x0300 (`02030000` at row 3). Table-type (with or without `Index`, identical under different indexes): byte 0 is the slot within a data page and byte 1 the page (row 1 of the 2048-row table = `00 3e`, row 255 = `02 4c`, 256 = `03 4c`, 1000 = `09 77`, last = `0d b5`; small table pages `2d`). Revisiting a row returns equal bytes; the bytes are unchanged by a key change and by growing a row to 255 characters. After an external DELETE of the current row, assigning the earlier-read bookmark of that row succeeds, keeps BOF/EOF false and `AbsolutePosition`, and field reads give 3167; a bookmark for an ordinal past the end gives 3159, after which there is no current row (field read 3021); on a snapshot assigning the row's own bookmark succeeds. A retained bookmark survives `Requery` as long as its row is populated again: after `Requery` followed by `MoveLast` or by `Move 2` the earlier bookmark of ID 3 is accepted (ID 3, `AbsolutePosition` 2); with no population after `Requery` (`RecordCount` 1) the same assignment gives 3159 and no current row; so validity follows population, not `Requery`. Empty array and `""` succeed and move nothing; four zero bytes give 3159 and no current row; Long, Null and Empty give 3421 and keep the position; a bookmark of another recordset type gives 3159. A held recordset Field after DELETE of its row reads 3167, still 3167 after reinsertion of the same key (the new row is not shown), after `Move 0`, and when a walk reaches that slot; after `Requery` it reads the first row. `LastModified` is null on a fresh recordset and equals `Bookmark` after Update. `Index` switch keeps the current row as the first row of the new index.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; bookmarks assigned as constants (a captured SAFEARRAY cannot be passed back).
- **Records.** q2/id-bookmark-requery-populated (discriminating: four Requery variants in one sequence, with and without population), id-bookmark-number-map; q/id-bookmark-bytes-by-row (ordinals 255/256/257/1000/2048 on three cursors), id-bookmark-assign-deleted-and-beyond, id-bookmark-after-requery-and-reinsert, id-held-field-after-delete-reinsert; c1/id-bookmark-shape, id-key-change, id-relocation, id-delete-reinsert, id-index-switch, id-field-held; fix/id-bookmark-assign-fresh; c1b/bookmark-walk-types, clone-bookmark-sharing, lastmodified-addnew.
- **Open.** The cause of the four +59 jumps is not identified (the book does not require matching bookmark bytes). (T) Relocation confirmation, split, allocation reuse, file-copy restore (bytes).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-SEEK: operators follow index order; short and omitted keys are prefix bounds; duplicates select the first; long keys do not collide up to 255 characters

- **Claim.** On a table-type recordset with `Index` set, `=`, `>=`, `<`, `<=` and `>` select by index order, not value order (a descending component swaps `<` and `>`). With duplicates `=`, `>=` and `<=` select the first duplicate and `>`/`<` the row after/before the group, also across leaves. Short keys (fewer values than index columns) act as a prefix: `=`, `>=` and `>` all land on the FIRST row whose prefix is at or after the key (so `>` behaves like `>=`), and `<` and `<=` both land on the last row before that group, or NoMatch with no current row if none precedes; identical from a first-row and a last-row start. On IX_ABC (A asc, B desc, C asc): key (1): `=`,`>=`,`>` give (1,3,1), `<`,`<=` NoMatch; (2): `=`,`>=`,`>` give (2,3,1), `<`,`<=` give (1,1,2); (1,2): (1,2,1) and (1,3,2); (2,1): (2,1,1) and (2,2,2); full key (1,2,1): `=`,`>=`,`<=` (1,2,1), `>` (1,2,2), `<` (1,3,2). A `Missing` component truncates the key at that position (leading Missing = empty key = first row, middle = prefix before it); `Null` and `Empty` are real key values, except for a Yes/No component (below): `=` with one gives NoMatch (no current row), `>=` lands at the position of Null (lowest in an ascending component, last in a descending one: (1,Null,1) `>=` gives (2,3,1), (1,2,Null) `>=` gives (1,2,1)). Text keys of 100, 250, 254 and 255 characters that differ only near the end are distinct entries: each full key finds its own row, `>`/`<`/`>=` follow plain string order; a 250-character prefix `=` gives NoMatch and `>=` the first longer key. A text index ignores case and a trailing blank (`abc`, `ABC`, `"abc "` land together); `=""`, `="*"` and a Long key give NoMatch. A Date key must carry its time part. Refusals: `<>` and every other operator spelling give 3001 (Null operator 3421), no key 3001, more keys than columns 3250 (13 keys; 14 give 3001), non-numeric text for a Long key 3421 (text `x` for a Yes/No key too; the number 2 for it is True), `Null` or `Empty` for a Yes/No key 3162 under every operator, leading or trailing, ascending or descending, no `Index` 3019, dynaset 3251. The operator is checked before the keys (`<>` with a Yes/No Null is 3001), the keys before their count (a Yes/No Null with one key too many is 3162, not 3250). A refusal leaves the start row current, or no current row and NoMatch True after a miss. `Null` sorts first (`IX_K`: `=,Null` lands on the Null row; an IGNORE NULL index gives NoMatch). By-reference operator and key arguments (`Seek(ByRef "=", ByRef 3)`) behave as by-value (q2/byref-dao-move-seek).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; IX_ABC fixture of 13 rows, start rows chosen so no landing equals a start, plus the two-start repair fixtures.
- **Records.** q/seek-short-keys-from-first, seek-short-keys-from-last (discriminating: identical landings from two starts), seek-omission-positions, seek-long-key-collision; c1/seek-operators, seek-duplicates, seek-duplicates-multileaf; fix/seek-arguments-from-two-starts, seek-duplicates-null-from-last, seek-operator-spelling-controls; c1b/seek-gap-keys, seek-text-index, seek-date-index, seek-repeat-sequence; fix/index-metadata-seek-pk-name; r-sort-3b/mix-seek-b (Yes/No Null, 52 Seeks over three indexes), r-cursor-1-seek-keys/yes-no-null-key (discriminating: Empty, after a miss, with the operator, count and conversion refusals). Reconciles c1b/seek-three-column-mixed (partial `>` like `>=`; NoMatch exactly when no row precedes the group).
- **Open.** (T) Linked/ODBC and replication cases, binary and other index types. Whether a conversion failure (3421) also precedes the key count (3250) is not asked.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-SEEK (long keys): a Text key is the whole argument, and a key past 510 bytes takes the stored long-key finish, full or partial

- **Claim.** A Text or Memo Seek argument is not cut to the 255 units an index entry keeps: it is encoded whole, less its trailing U+0020 blanks, under the column's sort descriptor. So on a one-column Text(255) index `=` with A×256, A×257, A×1000, A×5000, a×256 or A×256 + blank is NoMatch, and `>=`/`>` land on the first entry after the A×255 group, `<=`/`<` on its last; A×254 + `Bx` lands between A×254 B and A×254 C (`=` NoMatch); A×255 followed by one or three blanks finds the A×255 group; 300 blanks find the empty value. Any Seek key, complete or of the first fields only, that is longer than 510 bytes is then finished like a stored key (II-05 §6: the first 508 bytes and the two-byte digest of the rest, final byte included) and compared as bytes. A complete key therefore finds its own row; a shortened partial key is a prefix of no entry, so `=` is NoMatch, `>=` and `>` land together on the first entry at or after its digest, and `<=` and `<` on the entry before, among the entries sharing its first 508 bytes: on LK by (K, N), é×255 alone lands after both é×255 rows (`>=` ID 29, `<=` ID 19, while (é×255, 1) is 19 and (é×255, 2) is 20 in digest order 20, 19), ß×255 between them (`>=` 26, `<=` 24), Han×255 too (`>=` 34, `<` 25); on (K, N DESC) the landings move with the digests (é×255 `>=` 30, ß×255 `>` 33). Keys at and below the cut (A×253 to A×255, é×127/128, ß×128, the supplementary×127) are ordinary prefix bounds.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB and MDB (General), table-type recordsets; r-sort-3-keys and r-sort-3b-memo fixtures.
- **Records.** r-cursor-1-seek-keys/long-partial-keys-accdb, -mdb (discriminating: all five operators on two directions, with controls below the cut), over-length-text-keys (discriminating: trailing blanks against a cut, blanks inside the first 255 units, lengths 256 to 5000); r-sort-3/keys-seek-accdb, -mdb, seek-accdb (U table, A×256); r-sort-3b/memo-text255-key.
- **Open.** A length limit for a Seek argument beyond 5000 units; a long partial key on a Memo index.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-SEEK (miss): a Seek that finds nothing keeps BOF and EOF and parks the cursor beyond the end of its direction

- **Claim.** After a Seek that finds nothing, NoMatch is True, there is no current row (Field.Value, Move 0 and Bookmark give 3021) and BOF and EOF keep the values they had before the Seek (False/False from a current row, EOF True from EOF). For `=`, `>=` and `>` the cursor is after the last row: MovePrevious lands on the last row; MoveNext sets EOF without an error, or gives 3021 when EOF was already True. For `<` and `<=` it is before the first row: MoveNext lands on the first row; MovePrevious sets BOF without an error (EOF keeps its value), or gives 3021 when BOF was already True. A miss inside the order (`=` 1,25 between existing keys) behaves as a miss past the end, not as an insertion point. NoMatch is cleared only by a move that lands on a row, MoveFirst or MoveLast; a move that only sets EOF or BOF leaves it True. Setting `Index` moves to the first row of the new index.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, table-type recordset, PK_C (A ASC, B DESC) over four rows; the steps share one recordset, so each Seek starts from the previous step's state.
- **Records.** w-04-key-navigation/seek-miss-position (discriminating: a middle miss, both end misses, every move from each, flags from a current row and from EOF); c1/seek-arguments (NoMatch from a current row, then MoveNext), id-index-switch (Index moves to the first row).
- **Open.** Move n (n not 0) from a miss; a miss on an empty table.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EDIT: edit buffer lifecycle, validation order, AutoNumber reservation, FK and Required-Memo edits

- **Claim.** `Edit` gives EditMode 1, `AddNew` 2 (generated AutoNumber read at AddNew, defaults shown, other fields Null), `Update` and `CancelUpdate` 0; a second `Edit` reverts the buffer to the stored value; a second `Update` or `CancelUpdate` gives 3020. `Move`, `Requery`, `FindFirst`, `GetRows`, a Bookmark assignment and `Close` discard a pending edit; `Clone`, `Filter`, `Sort`, `OpenRecordset`, `RecordCount` and external writes keep it. A violation at `Update` leaves EditMode and the buffer; check order: Required/NOT NULL (3314) before the field rule (3316) before the unique index (3022); the table rule gives 3316; assignment order does not change it. `ValidateOnSet True` raises 3316 at the assignment, EditMode stays 1. Relationship violations at `Update` leave EditMode and buffer: a child with a missing parent 3201 (AddNew EditMode 2, Edit EditMode 1), a parent key change or Delete with children 3200 (Delete leaves the current row and EditMode 0); the stored tables are unchanged. A Required Memo made with DDL `NOT NULL`: `Update` with the Memo unset gives 3314 (EditMode 2) and again with Null, a zero-length string is accepted and stored (a Required zero-length Text gives 3315, fix/con-zero-length-required-failonerror). AutoNumber: the value is reserved at `AddNew` and is never reused: `AddNew`+`CancelUpdate` (1), `AddNew`+`Update` inside a transaction then `Rollback` (2), a new `AddNew`+`CancelUpdate` (3) and, after the Database is closed and reopened, the next `AddNew` gives 4, and only that row is stored. After `Update` the current row stays the old row; the new row's `LastModified` equals its `Bookmark`. After `Rollback` of the Workspace the recordset reads 3420 and `LastModified` is a null array. A successful `Update` leaves the previous `Errors` entry. Snapshot and forward-only refuse Edit/AddNew/Update/Delete 3251 and field set 3027.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; table-type and dynaset.
- **Records.** q/edit-addnew-counter-rollback-reopen (discriminating: cancel, rollback, reopen in one sequence), edit-fk-violation, edit-required-memo-addnew; c1/edit-addnew-generated, edit-buffer-lifecycle, edit-move-close-buffered, edit-validation-order, edit-cancel-rollback; fix/edit-validation-errors-per-failure; c1b/edit-pending-ops, editmode-by-type, editmode-unusual-transitions, update-arguments; def/assign-without-edit-by-type, field-value-trio-during-edit; fix/assign-without-edit-own-transaction.
- **Open.** Time/random/UDF defaults (R-EXPR-1/R-UDF-1). Probe defect: q/edit-conflict-other-row-and-restore and q/edit-required-memo-addnew call `CancelUpdate`/set after a successful `Update`, leaving a harmless 3020.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EDIT (conflicts): an optimistic Update is refused 3197 when the stored row image differs from the one read; a pessimistic edit locks other Workspaces

- **Claim.** Optimistic: A's first `Update` gives 3197 when B changed the same field or another field of the row (EditMode stays 1, buffer kept); a second explicit `Update` then succeeds and overwrites B's change. `Update` succeeds when B (second Database of the same Workspace) changed another ROW of the table (ID 3 while A edits ID 2), changed another field of the same row and restored it, ran `UPDATE T SET N = N` on the same row, or changed and restored the same field (def). So the conflict test compares the row image, not a modification counter. `dbSeeChanges` (512) makes `Edit` itself give 3197 when the row changed after the recordset was read. Pessimistic: B (second Workspace) `Edit` on A's locked row gives 3188 (also another row of the same table: the lock is page-granular, see ERR-PARTIAL); after A's `Update` B edits and updates.
- **Conditions.** Two Databases of one Workspace (optimistic); second appended Workspace (pessimistic); dynaset; ACCDB; rows of the 8-row table share one page.
- **Records.** q/edit-conflict-other-row-and-restore (discriminating: (c) other row, (f2) restore of another field, (f3) no-op update); fix/edit-conflict-optimistic-readback, edit-conflict-pessimistic-second-workspace; def/edit-conflict-restore-value, field-value-trio-other-writer; c1b/option-seechanges; e1b/lock-optimistic-conflict, lock-pessimistic-delete; q2/errpart-lock-multipage-* (page granularity).
- **Open.** (T) (d) body-only relocation and (e) delete/reinsert incarnation need native bytes to confirm the condition; whether "other row" holds on a different page (by the page-granular lock of ERR-PARTIAL it need not).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-PROV: updatability and provenance by source shape; edits reach the current row; an omitted Type cell changes option 4

- **Claim.** Dynaset `Updatable` is True for a plain table, DISTINCTROW, TOP and inner/LEFT joins; False for DISTINCT, GROUP BY, UNION and TRANSFORM (edit 3027, Update 3020, crosstab `Requery` 3251); every snapshot is not updatable. `DataUpdatable` is True for base fields and aliases, False for expressions and aggregates (`SourceTable` empty for aggregates; an expression keeps the first referenced column as `SourceField`). Two aliases of one source field update together. In a join a parent-field edit through one child row shows in the other child row and cursor; a child-only edit changes only that child; the join key can be set or refused (3101 missing parent, 3164 parent key). On the null-extended row of a LEFT JOIN a child edit fails 3058 (EditMode stays 1) and a parent edit succeeds; `AddNew` with only the absent side fails 3101. `AddNew` with only parent fields creates a parent row, with only child fields 3164 (omitted/consistent); under option 16 child fields are assignable and referential integrity still refuses (3200, 3201, 3022); `Delete` removes the child row only or fails 3200 (option 16). Two equal heap rows, and a projection WITHOUT the key over two equal rows: an `Edit`/`Update` or `Delete` with the cursor on the SECOND of the two changes that second row on dynaset and on table-type; the identity is the current row, not a lookup by value. Options: a writable open gives `Updatable` 0 for option 4 on table-type and dynaset when Type is given (`("T",1,4)`, `("T",1,4,Missing)`, `("T",2,4)`, named `Type:=1, Options:=4`; Edit gives 3027 "read-only"); when the Type cell is omitted, by `Missing` (`("T",Missing,4)`) or by naming only `Options:=4`, the result is a writable table-type (Type 1, `Updatable` True, Edit works); `("T",Missing,4,3)` gives 3001; option 8 gives RecordCount 0; 256 not bookmarkable; 16|32 gives 3001.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, writable opens (the read-only opens of def/prov-* field metadata are void and repeated in fix).
- **Records.** q/prov-heap-second-row-edit, prov-missing-key-second-row-edit (discriminating: cursor moved to the second equal row), prov-readonly-option-forms; q2/named-dao-order-variants (the omitted-Type case through a named argument, `Options:=4` alone and `Type:=1, Options:=4`); fix/pos-query-sources-rw, field-metadata-rw, prov-sources-errors-per-call, prov-outer-join-cancel-after-failed-update, option-flags-rw, option-join-addnew-delete-omitted/inconsistent/consistent, updatable-members-rw; def/prov-alias-edit-duplicate-source, prov-parent-edit-two-children, prov-join-field-change, prov-inconsistent-referential-integrity; c1b/option-join-updatability, option-append-only.
- **Open.** Why an omitted Type cell drops the read-only effect of option 4 (observed on two routes, not explained; an ACE quirk the adapter must reproduce). Linked-source identity (outside the local scope of this card).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-BULK: GetRows array shape, counts, short return, deleted rows, edit interplay

- **Claim.** `GetRows` returns a `VT_ARRAY|VT_VARIANT` `[0+fields, 0+rows]` with the stored types' cell tags (Long I4, Yes/No BOOL, Byte UI1, Integer I2, Currency CY, Double R8, Single R4, Date DATE, Text/Memo BSTR with the whole Memo, binary `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1`, Null `VT_NULL`, all-Null row `VT_BOOL 0` for Yes/No and `VT_NULL` elsewhere), identical on all four types. A Decimal(18,4) made by ADO DDL reads as DECIMAL scale 4. There is no row ceiling: `GetRows(1500)` returns 1500 rows on a dynaset and a snapshot (2048-row source), the next `GetRows(1000)` returns the remaining 548 with EOF true, and one more at EOF gives 3021. Count arguments: 0, -1 and True give 3001; digit strings and fractions convert (2.5 gives 2); text and Null 3421; a Missing count or no argument returns 1; on an empty table or at EOF 3021; by-reference counts (`GetRows(ByRef 2)`) behave as by-value. During `Edit`/`AddNew` `GetRows` moves and discards the edit (EditMode 0, then `CancelUpdate` 3020). Rows deleted by another call AHEAD of the cursor are skipped by `GetRows` and by `MoveNext` alike (rows 5 and 6 deleted: `GetRows(4)` from ID 1 returns IDs 1-4 and leaves the cursor on ID 7, `AbsolutePosition` 4; `MoveNext` over a deleted ID 2 lands on ID 3). With the cursor ON a row another call has deleted (dynaset at a deleted current row, a dynaset whose next rows were deleted, and table-type), `GetRows(n)` returns a degenerate array `[0+0,0+3]` with no cells and no error, the cursor stays on the deleted slot (BOF and EOF false, ID read 3167), and a repeated call returns the same. Local `CacheSize` accepts 0, -1, 1201 and `"10"` and reads 0; `FillCache` and `CacheStart` accept every argument; table-type `Cache*` give 3251 and `FillCache` 3219.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, local.
- **Records.** q2/bulk-getrows-cursor-on-deleted-row (discriminating: dynaset and table-type, own-row and following-rows deletions); q/bulk-getrows-large-count, bulk-getrows-deletion-discovery; q2/byref-dao-move-seek; c1b/getrows-count-arguments, getrows-beyond-eof, getrows-shape-types, getrows-deleted-row, getrows-edit-buffer, cache-size-properties, cache-fill-and-start; def/bulk-getrows-memo-binary; fix/getrows-decimal-ado-ddl.
- **Open.** (T) Large-value resource failures, ODBC CacheSize. The same degenerate shape `[0+0,0+2]` was seen for the DAO-made Decimal field (c1b), so it is ACE's answer when a row cannot be read, not specific to deletion.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-LIFE: Clone, Filter/Sort, Requery and Close relations of a recordset family; shared population; clone close in transactions

- **Claim.** A clone of a table, dynaset or snapshot starts on the first row with its own position and bookmark; the parent's moves do not move it; forward-only cannot be cloned (3219); `Clone(4)`, `Clone(2)` and Null give 3001. A dynaset bookmark is accepted by its clone and a separate dynaset (same row) and survives the parent's `Close`; a bookmark of another recordset type gives 3159. A clone does not inherit Filter or Sort. Population is shared by the family: a clone made after the parent's `MoveLast` reports `RecordCount` 2048 at once (`AbsolutePosition` 0); a clone made before population reports 1 as does the parent, and after either `MoveLast` both report 2048. A clone's `Update` sets its `LastModified`, not the parent's, and the reverse; a clone's edit is seen in the parent at once on the same row, and a clone's Delete reads 3167 in the parent. A clone and a derived recordset survive the parent's `Close`; a closed object gives 3420 for every member including `Close`. `Filter`/`Sort` change nothing in the recordset; `OpenRecordset()` derives a dynaset (snapshot from a snapshot), always `Updatable` False, with errors at the derived open. `Requery` on dynaset and snapshot returns to row 1 with `RecordCount` 1; held clones and children then read 3021 until a move; table-type `Requery`, `Filter` and `Sort` give 3251. Clone close and transactions: closing a clone created INSIDE the explicit transaction has no effect on the transaction or the work (clone closed after Update then `Rollback`: row back; pending Edit stays EditMode 1 and a later Update and `CommitTrans` store it; after Update then `CommitTrans`: stored; nested inner Rollback, inner Commit then outer Rollback behave as ordinary transactions; no transaction: stored row unchanged), also with a second Database of the Workspace enlisted (an Update through each Database, a clone of each closed: `Rollback` restores both rows, `CommitTrans` stores both). Closing a clone created BEFORE `BeginTrans` while the transaction is open fails 3246 "Operation not supported in transactions" and ENDS the transaction: the update made inside it is lost (row unchanged), the following `Rollback` or `CommitTrans` gives 3034, in a one-level transaction after Update, after a pending Edit (`EditMode` reads 1 but a later `Update` gives 3020 and nothing is stored), and in a two-level transaction (one failing close ends both levels: both later calls give 3034).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, dynaset/snapshot/table; clone-in-transaction cases on a dynaset of T.
- **Records.** q2/life-clone-before-begintrans-rollback, life-clone-before-begintrans-commit, life-clone-before-begintrans-no-update, life-clone-before-begintrans-two-levels (discriminating: one element varied from q/life-clone-close-transaction-nested: the clone's creation before or inside the transaction), life-clone-close-second-database-enlisted; q/life-clone-population-order, life-clone-close-transaction-one-level, life-clone-close-transaction-nested; c1/life-clone, life-clone-close-transaction, life-filter-sort, life-requery, life-close-held; c1b/clone-by-type-and-properties, clone-chain-close-orders, clone-bookmark-sharing, lastmodified-clone-family, filter-*, sort-expression-forms, requery-types-state, requery-family; fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row; def/recordset-closed-member-inventory.
- **Open.** The before-BeginTrans case was run on a dynaset clone only; snapshot and table-type clones and a clone created before an outer transaction but closed inside a nested one are untried.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-BOOT: first touch creates the default Workspace; SystemDB is empty only before it; alias paths open separate Databases

- **Claim.** The first touch of the default session by `Workspaces`, `OpenDatabase` or `BeginTrans` each creates one workspace "#Default Workspace#", user "admin", type 2. `DBEngine.SystemDB` reads "" before the session exists and "system.mdb" after any touch; `Properties("SystemDB").Value` read as the first member gives "system.mdb" and the direct getter then agrees (so the d1b difference was read order). `DefaultUser("Admin")` set before first use gives `UserName` "Admin". After `Close` of the default Workspace `Count` is 1, the wrapper and `Name` still read, `OpenDatabase` still works, and the earlier Database gives 3420. `CreateWorkspace` does not append (`Count` unchanged) and the unappended workspace opens a database; a duplicate name appends and `Item(name)` returns the first; an empty name gives 3271. The same file opened twice in one Workspace gives two Database objects (`Count` 2, `Item(name)` the first), a third open exclusive succeeds. Alias spellings of the path (upper-case, forward slashes, dot segments) each open as a separate Database of the Workspace (Count 2, 3, 4), read the same data, and `Database.Name` returns the canonical path (original case, backslashes, dot segment resolved) for every spelling; the extended `\\?\` spelling is refused 3043 ("network access interrupted") and adds no member; the upper-case spelling also opens in a second appended Workspace. `Rollback`/`CommitTrans` without `BeginTrans` give 3034. `IniPath` reads "" and `DefaultType` 2.
- **Conditions.** One process per probe (first touch isolated); ACCDB/MDB; local path.
- **Records.** q2/boot-alias-path-open-twice; q/boot-systemdb-direct-first, boot-systemdb-properties-first (discriminating: the two read orders); d1e/boot-first-workspaces, boot-first-opendb, boot-first-begintrans, boot-close-default, boot-createworkspace, boot-open-twice, sec-logon-defaults; d1b/eng-members-each, eng-properties-enumerated, ws-members-default, ws-members-explicit.
- **Open.** (T) IniPath/DefaultType setting changes (settings), Access CurrentDb.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-CREATE: refusals leave no file; the engine-route Database is a member of Databases at once

- **Claim.** `Workspace.CreateDatabase` of an existing path gives 3204 with no file change, version 12345 gives 3001 with no file created, a call inside a transaction gives 3246 with no file created. After `DBEngine.CreateDatabase` a `Databases` collection fetched afterwards holds the new Database (Count 1, `Refresh` leaves 1), and a collection HELD before the call goes from Count 0 to 1 at once, without `Refresh`; `Workspace.CreateDatabase` of a second file gives Count 1 or 2 at once, members in creation order; closing a Database removes it (Count back, no `Refresh`). A created ACCDB has 5 system TableDefs (6 after a first CREATE TABLE and reopen) and is 172032 bytes. A database returned by `CreateDatabase` is held exclusively by its process; a peer's open answers 3024 before the file exists and opens after the creator closes it. A freeze of the creating process 5 ms and 40 ms after the call started (9 threads suspended) found no destination file yet (peer open 3024); after resume the file exists and a peer opens it read-only with 5 TableDefs.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; default Workspace; file observation by the harness.
- **Records.** q2/create-held-databases-before-engine-create (discriminating: collection held before the engine-route call), create-suspend-mid-call-early, create-suspend-mid-call-late; q/create-membership-refresh; d1e/create-workspace-route.
- **Open (F).** (1) d1e/create-workspace-route read a held Databases count of 0 after a successful `eng.CreateDatabase` made with `;LANGID=0xFFFF;CP=1;COUNTRY=0` (after earlier failed creates in the same session); the plain create does not reproduce it: one probe with that locale string. (2) Both freezes landed before the destination existed (creation plus close took about 1 s): a later freeze, between roughly 100 and 900 ms, is needed to see a partial file. (T) Failure at acquisition/transfer/flush/publication boundaries.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-ID: wrapper identity on repeated retrieval; held TableDef across Delete versus SQL drop and recreate

- **Claim.** Retrieving the same Workspace, Database, TableDef, Field, Index, Relation, QueryDef, Parameter, Property or Document twice returns one identity (canonical-IUnknown token); a second parent binding returns the same Parameters and Parameter and a value set through one is seen through the other; two `CoCreateInstance` calls of the DBEngine class in one process return the same object (r-ops/activation-routes). A held TableDef survives rename and SQL `DROP TABLE` for `Name` and `Fields`. After a SQL `DROP TABLE` and `CREATE TABLE` of the same name the held wrapper reads the NEW table (Name 'Rc2', `Fields.Count` 2 against the original 1) before and after `Refresh`, while `tds.Count` is stale until `Refresh`. A TableDef removed by `TableDefs.Delete` gives 3420 at once for `Name` and `Fields`, still 3420 after `Refresh` and after a table of the same name is recreated, and also after `Rollback` of that delete; `Item(name)` then returns a new object reading the new schema. A held recordset Field follows the current row across moves, a failed `FindFirst` and `Requery`.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB.
- **Records.** q/id-recreate-same-name-held (discriminating: Delete route and SQL route in one sequence); d1e/id-twice, id-held-rename; fix/deleted-tabledef-held-calls; c1/id-field-held; ops/activation-routes.
- **Open.** (T) VBA `Is`, default-member DISPID 0, forced native page reuse. Probe defect: q/id-recreate-same-name-held never recorded the original `Fields.Count` of Rc2 before the drop (1 is taken from the DDL).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-COLL: collection lookup, ordinal coercion, staleness and mutation rules

- **Claim.** Name lookup is case-insensitive in every family; composed and decomposed accents match; trailing or leading blanks, embedded NUL, a missing name and an empty name give 3265. Ordinals: I2, UI1, R8 (0, 0.5), BOOL 0 and DATE give the item by index with R8 rounded to nearest even (1.5 and 2.5 both give index 2); digit strings, BOOL -1, Null, Empty, Missing and out-of-range give 3265; an object argument gives two Errors entries (3001, 3421; also on QueryDefs, Relations, Fields, Indexes, Properties). A missing name in `Properties.Item` gives 3270 (0x800A0CC6) and in `Properties.Delete` 3265. Counts and members of TableDefs, Indexes (after `Relations.Append`), Documents and a second Database's QueryDefs stay stale until `Refresh`; a cross-Database `QueryDefs` refresh appends new members after the alphabetical block; index order is not creation order. `Append`/`Delete` on Containers, Documents, Databases and Errors do not exist; `Workspaces.Delete` 0x800A0CC9; `Fields.Delete` of an indexed field 0x800A0CD0, of a relation field 0x800A0CE7, of an unindexed field succeeds at once; `Indexes.Delete` of a relation's primary index 0x800A0CD1; `OrdinalPosition` puts are accepted without renumbering siblings. `Refresh` succeeds on 17 collections. `_NewEnum` walks the same members in the same order (see DAO-ENUM).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB (MDB for the d1b matrix).
- **Records.** d1e/coll-name-variants, coll-tabledefs-index-variants, coll-mutation-refresh; d1b/coll-item-top-level, coll-item-table-family, coll-item-query-family, coll-refresh-each, coll-append-delete-unsupported, coll-delete-field-indexed, coll-delete-index-in-relation, coll-fields-ordinalposition, coll-querydefs-cross-db-refresh; fix/coll-delete-field-unindexed, coll-append-field-readback; def/fields-lookup-forms-on-recordset; q/errorder-dispatch-ordinal-by-collection; q2/enum-dao-top-level-collections.
- **Open.** External-process mutation; legacy `Connection.QueryDefs` (ODBCDirect not in ACE).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-ENUM: `_NewEnum` returns an IEnumVARIANT over the live, stale-until-Refresh membership; Skip(1) at the end is E_FAIL

- **Claim.** Every DAO collection tried answers `_NewEnum` (VT_UNKNOWN) and `IEnumVARIANT` (S_OK): Workspaces, Databases, Users and Groups (empty), TableDefs, QueryDefs, Relations, Containers, Properties, Errors, and the child collections Fields and Indexes of a TableDef, IndexFields, a Recordset's Fields, a Relation's Fields, a Container's Documents, a QueryDef's Parameters. Items come in the collection's own order (TableDefs alphabetical with the MSys tables, Documents likewise with saved queries; Properties in built-in order), each `Next(1)` returns one object; `Next` past the end returns S_FALSE (0x00000001, 0 fetched), `Reset` S_OK and `Next` after `Reset` the first item again, `Clone` S_OK. `Skip(1)` after `Reset` and one `Next` returns S_OK when more members follow (TableDefs, Containers, Properties, Fields, Documents, Parameters) and E_FAIL (0x80004005) when the collection has one member or none (Workspaces, Databases, QueryDefs, Relations, Errors, Users, Groups, Indexes, IndexFields, Relation Fields): a skip that would pass the end fails. The enumerator walks the held collection's CURRENT membership, which is stale until `Refresh`: a table created during the walk (Count 12 until `Refresh`, 13 after) is not returned; a table dropped by SQL during the walk is still returned (15 members, `Name` readable) and the Count falls only after `Refresh`; `Refresh` during the walk does not invalidate the enumerator and a table created by another Database before the walk and made visible by that `Refresh` is returned at the end (13 members, appended last). After `Database.Close` during a walk the enumerator's `Next` returns the raw error HRESULT 0x800A0D5C (3420), `Reset` S_OK, `Skip` E_FAIL, `Clone` S_OK, and the collection's own members give 3420.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; default Workspace; items read through each enumerator's `Name` (Number for Errors).
- **Records.** q2/enum-dao-top-level-collections, enum-dao-child-collections (discriminating: single-member against multi-member collections for `Skip`), enum-mutation-add-during-walk, enum-mutation-remove-during-walk, enum-refresh-during-walk, enum-close-during-walk; ops/enum-dao-collections.
- **Open.** Refresh that REMOVES a member while an enumerator is live; enumeration across a second Workspace. Probe defect: the add/remove probes call a failing statement in every later `Next` body (3010, 3376), which is harmless noise.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-DRAFT: drafts name the failing call, leave counts unchanged and can be corrected; attached setters; transactional appends

- **Claim.** `TableDefs.Append` of a draft without fields gives 3264; `CreateField(name, 9999)` fails in the constructor 3259; `CreateTableDef(Null)` 3421; an unnamed Field and a duplicate-named Field append to a draft; a Field object appended twice or to a second TableDef gives 3367; invalid table names (`!`, `.`, brackets, leading blank, control characters, empty) give 3125 at `Append` (a `Name` put of `bad!name` on a draft is accepted, the failing call is the parent `Append`), 65 characters fail in `CreateTableDef` 3001; index append: no fields 3264, missing field 3409, second primary 3283, duplicate name 3284; relation append: missing table 3011, no fields 3366; `CreateQueryDef("")` creates "#Temporary QueryDef#", a named query appends at once, duplicate 3012, bad SQL 3129, missing table succeeds; renaming a temporary query or appending it gives 3219. `Count` is unchanged after every refusal. A draft is correctable: TableDef Dr1 (no fields, 3264) appends after a field is added; Dr2 appends after its `Name` is put back; an Index draft appends after `CreateField`+`Fields.Append`; a Relation draft (3366) appends after its field with `ForeignName` is added; counts are stale until `Refresh` (Indexes 1 to 2, Relations 1 to 2). On attached objects: `TableDef.Name` and `Field.Name` puts and `Field.Required` put are accepted and read back, `Field.Size` put on a Text field gives 3219. Inside a transaction `TableDefs.Append` of a draft succeeds (Count +1), `Rollback` leaves the Count stale until `Refresh`, after which the table is gone (`Item` 3265); `Fields.Append` on an attached TableDef in a transaction is rolled back the same way (Count 3 to 2 after `Refresh`).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; default Workspace transactions.
- **Records.** q/draft-correct-after-failure, draft-setters-after-attach-txn (discriminating: failure then correction in four object kinds; append then rollback); d1e/draft-tabledef-field, draft-index-relation, draft-querydef, err-invalid-names.
- **Open.** (T) Interrupted publication (R-CRASH-1; the sampling recorder covers INSERT page writes, not DDL). Probe defect: q/draft-setters-after-attach-txn did not read the renamed table back through `TableDefs.Item('Sa2')`, and the `Required` put was not checked by a reopen. File growth after read-write opens with no write (d1e) is unexplained.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-QUERY: parameter values, resolution point, SQL rewrite and removed parameters

- **Claim.** An untouched Parameter is `VT_EMPTY`; setting Empty reads `VT_I4 0`; Null reads `VT_NULL`; "abc" for a Long gives 3421 and the value stays; "2" reads `VT_I4 2`. A parameter query opened by `OpenRecordset` or run by `Execute` without values gives 3061; `Execute` on a select 3065. `Parameters.Append` and `Parameters.Delete` do not exist (0x80020006). Setting identical SQL keeps the held Parameter; renaming, retyping or reordering declarations makes the held Parameter give 3420; syntax-error SQL gives 3157 and keeps the old SQL. Removing a declaration (SQL put with only pA) leaves the held pA valid with its value (1), the held pB gives 3420 and `Parameters.Count` is 1; declaring pB again does not revive the held pB (3420), `Count` is 2, and a new `Item('pB')` is a new object whose Value is Empty. QueryDef Parameters enumerate in declaration order (pA, pB) with Name and Type (q2/enum-dao-child-collections).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, saved QueryDef.
- **Records.** q/query-param-remove-reintroduce; d1e/query-parameter-values, query-sql-rewrite; d1b/parameter-members-each, querydef-state-after-execute; r-cat-1b/qd-execute-param-action; q2/enum-dao-child-collections.
- **Open.** (T) `Recordset.Requery` on a parameter query is an access violation (c1b/requery-parameter-query), Requery argument forms, nested saved queries (R-SQL), pass-through.

### R-DAO-1 DAO-PROP: user and built-in Property lifecycle

- **Claim.** Properties enumerate without reading `Value` (Database 12 entries, TableDef 12, Field at least 17; the Database collection enumerates Name, Connect, Transactions, Updatable, CollatingOrder, QueryTimeout, Version, RecordsAffected, ReplicaID, DesignMasterID, Connection); `Inherited` per entry. `CreateProperty`+`Append` works for types 1-12 and 20 with typed values; a draft with `Value("")` or Null gives 3385 and `Append` before a value 3386; duplicate append 3367; `Type` put on an appended Property 3219; a Value put reads back converted to the property type; after `Delete` the held Property gives 3420 on `Name` and `Value`, a second `Delete` 3265, a recreated property of the same name is a new object (held one stays 3420); user, field and database property changes persist across reopen. Built-in properties cannot be deleted (0x800A0D38). Direct getters equal `Properties.Item(...).Value` for 8 members; user properties exist only in `Properties`. Wrong context: unknown name 3270, method form of a property 3251.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB.
- **Records.** fix/prop-user-appended-lifecycle; d1e/prop-enumerate-and-context; d1b/property-append-all-types, db-direct-vs-properties-item, coll-delete-builtin-property, db-properties-enumerated-*; q2/enum-dao-top-level-collections.
- **Open.** Which queries inherit the table Description; the trailing NUL in `ValidationRule` (d1b/field-members-typed-each).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-META: Containers and Documents are the three native containers; Permissions refuse until a put is attempted; held Document across drop and recreate

- **Claim.** Containers are exactly Databases, Relationships and Tables; Tables lists every table (including MSys tables) and saved query as a Document; counts stay stale until `Refresh` after a table CREATE/DROP or `CreateQueryDef`; `Owner` is "" and `UserName` "admin"; `Name`, `Container`, `DateCreated`, `LastUpdated` read. Container and Document `Permissions` and `AllPermissions` give 3358 (0x800A0D1E "Cannot open the workgroup information file") on every read made BEFORE a put has been tried (5 reads on a Document, 2 on a Container). A `Permissions` put (0 or 1048575) fails 3358; afterwards `Permissions` and `AllPermissions` read `VT_I4 0` without error, on the Document and on the Container, even after a second failed put, while `Errors` keeps 3358. A held Document after SQL `DROP TABLE` still reads its `Name` until `Documents.Refresh`, then gives 3420; after the table is recreated under the same name it stays 3420 even after `Refresh`; `Item(name)` is a new Document with a new `DateCreated`.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB and MDB (permissions); ACCDB (Document lifecycle).
- **Records.** q2/meta-permissions-put-then-read (discriminating with q/meta-document-permissions-repeat: reads before the put fail, after it return 0; reproduces d1e/sec-default-admin-*); q/meta-document-permissions-repeat, meta-held-document-recreate; d1e/meta-containers-documents; d1b/containers-*, documents-*, documents-lookup-by-live-names; r-cat-1b/containers-membership-after-ddl.
- **Open.** (T) Forms, reports, macros, modules (Access).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-SEC: the default-Admin context in ACE

- **Claim.** Without a workgroup file ACE runs as default Admin with a blank password: `CreateWorkspace` with a named user, or Admin with a wrong password, gives 3028; `Users` and `Groups` are empty on ACCDB and MDB (Count 0, an empty enumeration; `Item("Admin")` 3265); `CreateUser` succeeds without changing the file bytes; `Permissions`/`AllPermissions` refuse 3358 until a put has been tried (DAO-META); `Container`/`Document` metadata reads work.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB and a native MDB made by `CreateDatabase`; no workgroup file (`SystemDB` "system.mdb" is reported but not present).
- **Records.** d1e/sec-default-admin-accdb, sec-default-admin-mdb, sec-logon-defaults; d1b/coll-item-top-level; q/meta-document-permissions-repeat; q2/meta-permissions-put-then-read, enum-dao-top-level-collections.
- **Open.** An MDB that needs only the default workgroup, a write that would change protected security metadata (CalcDb-side NotModelled refusal: N; workgroup success excluded by the card).

### R-DAO-1 II-05 index slice: metadata, flags, DistinctCount (Null counts), nonindexable types, refusal precedence

- **Claim.** `PK_*` indexes are Primary, Unique and Required; Foreign indexes carry the Foreign flag; index order in `Indexes` is not creation order. `DistinctCount` counts distinct index entries and Null is one entry in an ordinary index: Q = 1,2,2,3,Null gives 4, and 4 for one Null among 5 rows with IgnoreNulls (the Null row is not in that index). A held Index keeps its value after an INSERT until `Indexes.Refresh` (4 to 5); a fresh `TableDefs.Refresh`/`Item` shows 5 at once; the value reads 5 after `Rollback` of a later insert, after `Refresh`, and after reopen. A newly appended index reads 0 until `Indexes.Refresh`, then 1. DAO flag errors: second primary 3283, duplicate name 3284, no fields 3264, missing field 3409, `Required` over a column holding Null 3058, duplicate keys under a unique index 3022. An OLE (LONGBINARY) field cannot be indexed (3409 "Invalid field definition", by SQL and by DAO `Indexes.Append`, the index is not created) while a MEMO field is indexed without error by both routes (Indexes count 1 to 2 to 3). Construction refusal precedence: a `UNIQUE ... WITH DISALLOW NULL` index on (A,B) gives 3058 (Null) on tables holding (1,1),(1,1),(9,Null) and the same rows with the Null inserted first, where in key order the Null row sorts AFTER the duplicate; on (7,1),(7,1),(2,Null) (Null sorting first) in both insertion orders it also gives 3058; unique only gives 3022, DISALLOW NULL only gives 3058, and no index is left behind. So the Null refusal precedes duplicate detection regardless of key order and insertion order. `Index='PK_T'` then Seek works; a wrong index name 3800 (current row unchanged); Seek with 13 keys gives 3250, with 14 keys 3001.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB.
- **Records.** q2/index-null-sorts-after-duplicate (discriminating: Null after the duplicate in key order, two insertion orders, single-defect controls); q/index-distinctcount-dml, index-nonindexable-types, index-build-order-failure; fix/index-metadata-seek-pk-name; d1b/index-members-each, index-create-readback; r-cat-1b/index-dao-create-flags, index-members-ddl; c1/id-index-switch; fix/err-index-over-duplicates-two-pass.
- **Open.** (T) Raw statistics words, Calculated and Attachment fields (no DAO DDL).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-LIFE: explicit Close makes held children give 3420; clones and derived recordsets outlive their parent; the last Release frees the file

- **Claim.** After `Database.Close` every held child (TableDefs, TableDef, Field, Indexes, QueryDef, Parameters, Recordset, Property, Containers) gives 3420 on every member including `Name` and a second `Close`; after `Workspace.Close` its Databases and children give 3420 and the appended workspace leaves the collection; after `Recordset.Close` every member and held Field gives 3420 while its clone, child and derived recordsets keep working; a recordset opened from a QueryDef survives `qd.Close()` and `qd.SQL` reads; a pending Edit is discarded by `Database.Close`; `db.Close()` with an open recordset gives no error; a live enumerator's `Next` then returns 0x800A0D5C. Releasing the last reference of a Database that was opened exclusive read-write, WITHOUT `Close` (wrapper count 0), frees the file: another process opens it exclusive read-write at once with no error and reads, whether the engine object was also released or not; the lock file is no longer listed afterwards.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, default Workspace; the release case with two processes (participant b's answer is the evidence; both records ReturnedSuccess).
- **Records.** q/life-release-without-close-database, life-release-without-close-database-and-engine; q2/enum-close-during-walk; d1e/life-database-workspace-close, life-recordset-querydef-release; def/recordset-closed-member-inventory; c1/life-close-held; c1b/clone-chain-close-orders.
- **Open.** Concurrent/reentrant Close (DAO-CLOSE-OWN, T). Probe defect: the q release probes did not record file state while A still held the database, so the lock file's presence before the release is inferred from earlier records.

### R-ERR-1 ERR-BASE: DBEngine.Errors is one collection per engine; ADO Errors is per Connection; a failed or aggregated activation leaves no entry

- **Claim.** `DBEngine.Errors` is a single collection for the engine (all Databases and Workspaces): empty before any failure (56 probes), one entry after a failure whose Number, Description, Source, HelpContext and HelpFile equal the failing call's EXCEPINFO; a later failure replaces it (3078 then 3061: Count 1, the later description); a later success of any kind leaves the old entry; `Errors.Refresh` changes nothing; a second `CoCreate` in the same process returns the same engine object and Errors; a second process (own engine) starts with Count 0 and its failures do not appear in the first. Activation failures leave nothing in Errors: `CoCreateInstance` of DAO.DBEngine.120 with CLSCTX_LOCAL_SERVER fails 0x80040154, with in-process aggregation CLASS_E_NOAGGREGATION 0x80040110 (E_UNEXPECTED 0x8000FFFF when the process already holds an engine, ops record), and a following in-process activation succeeds with `Errors.Count` 0; the first member failure (`OpenDatabase` of a missing file, 3024) then gives Count 1. ADO: each Connection has its own `Errors`: a provider failure on c1 (Count 1, SQLState 3000, NativeError) leaves c2 at 0; a different failure on c2 leaves c1's entry unchanged; a successful `Execute` on c1 clears c1 only (c2 keeps its entry).
- **Conditions.** DAO raw lane ACCDB; ADO read-only connections to one file.
- **Records.** q2/errbase-activation-failure-then-member (discriminating: activation failure separated from the first member failure); q/errbase-two-ado-connections (two live Connections, three steps); ops/activation-routes; e1b/proto-fail-success-no-read, proto-two-fails-no-read, proto-success-kinds, proto-errors-item-range; fix/proto-two-fails-different-numbers, err-base-scope-second-process (truly independent engine); d1e/err-base-scope.
- **Open.** The two aggregation answers differ between a fresh process (0x80040110, q2) and one that already holds an engine (0x8000FFFF, ops): one record each, order-dependent, outside this card's decision.

### R-ERR-1 ERR-BIND: positional, named and by-reference arguments

- **Claim.** Positional: arity errors on a DAO method give 3001 from the DAO source; an unknown member name fails in `GetIDsOfNames` with 0x80020006 (no member entry); a property called as a method or a put-ref on a get-only member gives 3251; for `OpenRecordset("T", Type, 0, 3)` a Type cell of Missing or Empty gives 3001 and Null 3421; "4" and R8 4.5 succeed as a snapshot (type 4); BOOL -1 gives 3001; swapped arguments give 3421; an out-of-range type (999) or `Execute` option 99999 gives 3001. Named: one `GetIDsOfNames` resolves the names and the call binds them by DISPID: `OpenRecordset` Name=0, Type=1, Options=2, LockEdit=3; `Execute` Query=0, Options=1; `Move` Rows=0; names are case-insensitive (`type` binds Type); any order works (`Type:=2, Name:='T'`); a name given both positionally and by name, or the same name twice, gives 3001; a wrong-typed named value gives the positional error (Type:='abc' 3421, Null 3421, Name:=Null 3421); a name the member does not have (`Execute` Name, `MoveNext` Rows, a property get with Index) fails in `GetIDsOfNames` with DISP_E_UNKNOWNNAME 0x80020006 and never enters the member. By reference: a DAO input parameter accepts a by-reference Variant and a typed by-reference I4 or BSTR (`OpenRecordset("T", ByRef 4)`, `(ByRef "T", 4)`, `Move(ByRef 2)`, `GetRows`, `FindFirst`, `Execute(ByRef sql, ByRef 128)`, `Seek(ByRef "=", ByRef 3)`) with the same result as by value and the value after the call unchanged; ByRef Empty gives 3001 and ByRef Null 3421 as by value. ADO writes back: `Connection.Execute(sql, RecordsAffected, options)` writes RecordsAffected into a by-reference variant or typed I4 (2 after an UPDATE of two rows, 0 after an UPDATE of none) and leaves a by-value argument alone.
- **Conditions.** Late-bound; DAO and ADO; the worker's by-reference and named-argument operations.
- **Records.** q2/named-dao-order-variants, named-dao-refusals, byref-dao-openrecordset, byref-dao-move-seek; ops/named-dao-openrecordset, byref-ado-execute-records-affected; d1e/err-bind-arity-member, err-bind-optional-cells, err-bind-types; e1b/arg-execute-options-99999, arg-openrs-type-999, arg-openrs-opentable-on-sql; d1b/eng-members-each; q/errinfo-presence-by-kind.
- **Open.** (T) Typed vtable and Access/VBA lanes; the managed lanes (ERR-MANAGED).

### R-ERR-1 ERR-DAO-ORDER: with several Errors entries the EXCEPINFO equals the last entry

- **Claim.** In every one of nine captures with more than one Errors entry the EXCEPINFO `scode` of the failing call equals the LAST entry: `Item(Object)` on TableDefs, QueryDefs, Relations, Fields, Indexes and Properties each gives [3001, 3421] with EXCEPINFO 3421, where entry 0 carries the fixed source `DAO.TableDefs` for all six and entry 1 the actual collection class (also the EXCEPINFO source); `Indexes.Append` on a held TableDef of a table dropped by SQL gives [3011, 3078] with EXCEPINFO 3078; an ODBC connection failure gives [0 (source `ODBC.Workspace`), 3146] with EXCEPINFO 3146 (retained record, not to be repeated). Entries run general first; `Errors.Refresh` changes nothing; each case follows a marker failure that left one entry; an enumerator over the collection returns the entries in the same order (q2/enum-errors-partial-consume).
- **Conditions.** DAO raw lane; the eight DAO-only captures are the qualifying ones.
- **Records.** q/errorder-dispatch-ordinal-by-collection (discriminating: six collections against a marker, DAO only); q2/enum-errors-partial-consume; d1e/err-dao-order, coll-tabledefs-index-variants; fix/deleted-tabledef-held-calls.
- **Open.** (T) Comparison with the VBA `Err` object (needs Access/VBA).

### R-ERR-1 ERR-ADO: which calls clear a Connection's Errors; Clear; provider versus runtime failures; no provider warning

- **Claim.** After a provider failure (Count 1, Number -2147217900 SQLState 3000, or -2147217865 SQLState 3078): a successful `Connection.Execute` clears `Errors` (Count 0); a property get (`State`) and `Recordset.MoveNext` on an open recordset leave it unchanged (Count 1); an ADO runtime failure (`Properties.Item` of a missing name, 3265-class) leaves Count 0; `Errors.Clear` empties it. Errors is scoped per Connection (ERR-BASE). A held Errors collection is live (Count follows the Connection) and a held Error object keeps its Number, Description and SQLState after `Clear` and after a later failure, while `Item(0)` afterwards returns a new object with the new failure. Requesting a recordset with a cursor and lock type ACE may downgrade (dynamic/optimistic, static/batch, forward-only/pessimistic) returns a keyset cursor (CursorType 1 in all three, LockType as requested), `Errors.Count` stays 0, and a recording sink on ConnectionEvents sees no InfoMessage (dispid 0 never raised); the events seen are WillConnect (6), ConnectComplete (7), WillExecute (4), ExecuteComplete (5) and Disconnect (8).
- **Conditions.** ADO, Provider Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0, read-only and read-write connection; event sink on the calling thread.
- **Records.** q/errado-clear-by-operation (discriminating: one call kind varied after the same failure), errado-held-after-clear, errbase-two-ado-connections; q2/errado-warning-infomessage; fix/ado-connection-errors-clear-with-entries; d1e/ado-connection-errors, ado-recordset-warning-runtime; ops/ado-connection-events.
- **Open.** (T) A successful provider warning has still not been observed (three downgrades, no InfoMessage, no Errors entry). Probe defect: in q/errado-clear-by-operation the runtime case clears Count while `cn.Properties()` (a get) alone does not, but the clearing call (`Item` failure versus the failed `Properties` object call) is not separated further.

### R-ERR-1 ERR-SNAPSHOT: a held Error is an immutable snapshot; the Errors collection and its enumerator are live

- **Claim.** DAO: a held `Error` object (e0 from failure A, 3078) keeps its Number and Description after failure B (3061), after a successful call, after `Errors.Refresh`, after A repeated, after `Database.Close` and after a failure of the Errors collection itself (`Item(1)`, 3265); the held `Errors` collection reads Count 1 throughout while a fresh `Item(0)` always shows the newest failure (3061, 3078, 3265); `eng.Errors()` returns the same collection wrapper. An `IEnumVARIANT` over Errors that is half consumed is live: with a two-entry collection [3001, 3421] and the first `Next` returned (3001), a new failure replaced the collection by one entry (3376); the next `Next` returned S_FALSE (the position was already past the single entry), `Reset` then `Next` returned the new entry, and the item already returned kept its payload. ADO: see ERR-ADO (held Error survives `Clear`).
- **Conditions.** DAO raw lane and ADO; ACCDB.
- **Records.** q2/enum-errors-partial-consume (discriminating: the collection replaced between two `Next` calls); q/errsnap-held-variants (six later events between two reads of one held object), errado-held-after-clear; d1e/err-snapshot-held.
- **Open.** None for the reference build; the book's "no universal snapshot semantics" remains: the policy is per family (Error immutable, Errors and its enumerator positional over the live collection).

### R-ERR-1 ERR-EXCEP: inline EXCEPINFO shape and which failures also publish an IErrorInfo

- **Claim.** Every failing DAO member returned `hr=0x80020009` with `wCode=0`, `scode=0x800A<number>`, `deferredFill=False`, `source` the class of the failing object, `helpFile` `jeterr40.chm` and `helpContext` 5000000+Number; VBA runtime errors (11, 5, 6) carry `helpFile` "-" and `helpContext` 4294967295, which the Error object reads as "" and -1. The public `Error.Number` is the scode low word. An `IErrorInfo` (guid zero, same source, description, help context) is published only for argument-count failures of Database and Recordset members (`OpenRecordset` with seven arguments or none: 3001; `MoveNext(1)`: 3001) and for `Field.Value` failures (put of "abc" into a Long: 3421; get of `1/0`: 11); it is absent for engine-service and collection failures (`Execute` on a missing table 3078, `TableDefs.Item('Nope')` 3265, `Item(Object)` 3421, `Recordset.Update` without Edit 3020, `DROP TABLE` on a missing table 3376) and for 3001 raised by an out-of-range VALUE of a correct-arity call (e1b/arg-execute-options-99999, arg-openrs-type-999). ADO failures carry EXCEPINFO `scode=0x80040E14`/`0x80040E37`/`0x80040E21` with source "Microsoft Access Database Engine" or `ADODB.*` and a populated IErrorInfo.
- **Conditions.** DAO raw lane, ACCDB; ADO provider routes.
- **Records.** q/errinfo-presence-by-kind (discriminating with e1b/arg-*: 3001 by arity has it, 3001 by value does not); e1b/* (56 probes, e.g. asg-byte-300, div0-select-read, fn-sqr-negative, missing-file); d1e/err-bind-arity-member; q2/errnest-ado-connection-event-error.
- **Open.** (T) A controlled deferred-fill object. Probe defect: the last case of q/errinfo-presence-by-kind (a member call on a closed recordset) was never run: the helper released the recordset, so the call hit an unbound target.

### R-ERR-1 ERR-NEST (ADO event part): completion events carry pError and adStatus and fire before the failing call returns

- **Claim.** On the calling thread, for `Connection.Execute`: WillExecute (dispid 4: source text, adStatus 1) then ExecuteComplete (dispid 5: RecordsAffected, pError, adStatus, pCommand, pRecordset, pConnection) then the call returns. For a successful Execute pError is null and adStatus 1; for a failing Execute (`SELEC ...` 0x80040E14, `SELECT * FROM NoSuchTable` 0x80040E37, also inside a transaction) pError is a non-null Error object and adStatus 2 (adStatusErrorsOccurred), the event fires before the call returns its failure, and `Connection.Errors` then holds the entry. `BeginTrans` raises dispid 1 (BeginTransComplete, level 1), `RollbackTrans` dispid 2 (the name of the event raised for a rollback is not resolved by the sink), `Close` dispid 8 (Disconnect). For a Recordset (keyset, optimistic): WillMove/MoveComplete (dispids 15/16) around Open and every move with adReason 10 (move) or 13/15; `AddNew` raises WillChangeRecord/RecordChangeComplete (11/12, reason 1), setting a field WillChangeField/FieldChangeComplete (9/10); a failing `Update` (duplicate primary key 0x80040E21) raises WillChangeRecord (reason 3) and RecordChangeComplete with a non-null pError and adStatus 2, `CancelUpdate` raises them again with reason 5, moves past the end raise EndOfRecordset (17), and the ADO error 3021-class (0x800A0BCD) follows on the extra `MoveNext`.
- **Conditions.** ADO, Provider Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0; the worker's recording sink (it records arguments and the thread, it cannot act inside a handler).
- **Records.** q2/errnest-ado-connection-event-error, errnest-ado-recordset-event-error, errado-warning-infomessage; ops/ado-connection-events.
